Grandfather Paradox Resolution via Accordance Principle

BothPhilosophicalLogicalResolution
Main paper §9.12.1 footnote 130

Ajax’s specific argument for how the grandfather paradox gets resolved in Aethic reasoning, laid out in footnote 130 with a particular position about which Aethic principle does the work. The straightforward argument—that the Third Postulate itself prunes the paradox because a successful grandfather-kill would be within the realm of contradictions the Third Postulate invalidates—is noted by Ajax as hypothetically possible, but it is not his personal preferred resolution. His personal intuition is that the Nexic Accordance Principle does the work earlier, before the Third Postulate ever gets a chance to act.

The argument structure: if one truly managed to go back in time to kill their grandfather, and the Third Postulate were the mechanism pruning all their attempts at success, then the question becomes why the Accordance Principle would allow such a string of miraculous interventions (failed attempts) to transpire in the first place. The probabilities of repeated miraculous blocks would multiply fiercely into extreme improbability. Instead of landing in a scenario where such probabilities must multiply, it would be far more likely that one never comes into contact with the time machine to begin with. The Accordance Principle therefore serves as a “pruning agent” earlier than the Third Postulate—it prevents one from engaging in the probabilistically risky behavior in the first place rather than intervening at each step of a doomed attempt.

Promoted to Tier B because the grandfather paradox is one of the most named problems in the philosophy of time travel, and Ajax’s specific two-layered resolution (Third Postulate works, but Accordance Principle works earlier and is his preferred reading) is a substantive contribution that reuses existing Nexic infrastructure. The same footnote is the origin of the Quantum Suicide/Immortality rebuttal in the main corpus, which is the direct application of the same “earlier-pruning-agent” logic to a different improbability-chain scenario.

414

Past-Aethus Perspective on Aethic Updates

AethicPhilosophicalOntologicalConcept
Main paper §2 footnote 22

An alternative framing of how Aethic updates work that Ajax introduces in footnote 22 as a more visual intuitive illustration of the Union Principle. Where centric unfolding always insists you view your block universe from your own perspective—moving forward through the Aethic graph as the experiencer—this alternative invites you to instead view your Aethic updating from a single fixed Aethus in your own Aethic past. Under this framing, when you observe one of two signs in a disagreeing superposition, you do not “cause” the other sign to disappear; rather, you “sort into the pocket” of the ladder ontology where you become permanently unable to interact with the second sign yourself. From your own current perspective your Aethus just updated; from the fixed past Aethus’s block universe, you simply moved into a different ladder-ontology layer where the second sign is still perfectly co-present with you as a physical “and” but is now permanently inaccessible to you specifically.

The deep insight Ajax draws from this alternative framing is a principle about temporal perspective: “The way you yourself view disagreeing superpositions that will be resolved in your Aethic future is how past versions of yourself view you currently.” Both vantage points are simultaneously valid, just indexed to different Aethae. Your current self’s prospective view of your future contains superpositions; your past self’s retrospective view of you contains those same superpositions, only resolved differently because your current Aethus has sorted into a specific pocket that your past self did not yet know about. This symmetry is what makes the framework categorically relational rather than merely epistemic. Promoted to Tier B because it also gives rise to an alternative derivation of the qualitative facet of the Extrusion Principle: if we trace the Aethus all the way back to the Null Aethus and then render every last disagreeing superposition in the block universe, what we get is “everything currently co-present with you physically,” which directly derives the qualitative-facet claim that different Aethic block universes share the same physical spacetime. The derivation is independent of the Extrusion Principle’s original motivation and converges on the same conclusion from a different starting point—a dual-edged constraint in miniature.

424

Emily and Sam Prague Reunion

AethicThought ExperimentPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §2

Ajax’s canonical showcase thought experiment for the anti-solipsistic “bustling multiverse” character of Aethic reasoning, and arguably the single most vivid illustration in the paper of why Aethic relativism does not collapse into classical idealism. The story: Emily grows up in the suburbs outside Boston, Massachusetts; at age twenty she travels Europe—Paris, London, Florence, Geneva—and in her last week in Prague meets Sam, the love of her life. By the end of their first conversation Emily learns the stunning coincidence: Sam had lived right next door to her childhood home in Massachusetts, and they had simply never met. Sam’s line: “we could have known each other from the beginning.” The paper warns upfront that engaging with this thought experiment requires “essentially unlearning everything you think you know about reality.”

The Aethic analysis runs through a deliberate perspective shift. Instead of looking at Emily’s past from her current Aethus in Prague (where she now knows Sam lived next door), we put ourselves back in Emily’s shoes as an eight-year-old standing outside her childhood home on a cold March morning twelve years earlier, waiting for her father to start the car. Staring at the house next door, young Emily is unaware of who lives there—she only learns it was Sam much later in Prague at age twenty. By the Second Postulate, a uniform random set of every possible human lives in that house at once from young Emily’s Aethus perspective. Ajax is careful to clarify the picture: this is not billions of potential humans being spatially crammed into the house, but rather an overlay. The point in spacetime that is the house’s living room is, from young Emily’s perspective, “essentially a bustling multiverse at which a vast density of timelines are bundled, all unbeknownst to one another.” For each possible family, there are pictures on the wall and laundry in the wash and food in the kitchen.

The anti-solipsism clarification is load-bearing. Sam very much does exist to young Emily’s Aethus, and he very much is in that house. Denying that would be epistemological solipsism on account of Emily actually meeting him twelve years later in Prague. What the Aethic framing is saying is specifically that the other possible families are in that house too, co-present with Sam, and the future random centric-unfolding to a particular reality in Prague is what selects which version lights up from Emily’s perspective. If young Emily walks to the door and knocks, a random person from the set of all families in Aethic superposition will answer, branching Emily into a new Aethus in which they were “the lone family in the house all along.” The likelihood that Sam is one of those people is essentially next to nothing—and yet the specific random future version of Emily in Prague will have had it be him. Promoted to Tier B because this is the paper’s most structurally complete non-quantum illustration of the Second Postulate and the clearest counterexample to the “Aethic reasoning is anti-realist” misreading.

310

Sequential Achievement Framework

MetaMathematicalEmpiricalTheorem
March 2026 paper—“Sequential Achievement and the Phase Transition in Outlier Productivity”

Root entry for Ajax’s March 2026 paper, which is a structurally independent contribution from the Aethic-Nexic framework but forms an important part of the overall research program. Central claim: complex achievements are well modeled as sequential stochastic processes requiring consecutive successes, and individual ability (score s) enters the process through a modified success probability in a manner that induces a sharp phase transition in expected completion time—from exponential in the sequence length for low-scoring individuals to approximately linear for high-scoring ones. The phase transition provides a mechanistic explanation for the extreme concentration of output among top performers and for the observation that individuals only modestly separated in latent ability can be separated by orders of magnitude in realized achievement. Calibrated against the Einstein 500-hours anchor and verified against Newton’s rank-#1 residual, it yields a critical score s* ≈ 6.4 and a depth n ≈ 7,700 for foundational theoretical advances. Independent of Aethic and Nexic reasoning but developed by Ajax within the same research program and sharing the type-theoretic notational style flagged in the paper’s §3.2.

C

Definitions, Operations & Supporting Concepts

The mathematical infrastructure, supporting operations, philosophical interpretations, and specific applied hypotheses that populate the framework beneath its foundational principles.
242

Boole-Jevons Historical Parallel

AethicMetaPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
§2.2.6 of main paper

Diagnostic of the length of time a conceptual reform of this specific structural shape historically takes to land. Boole’s original 1847 “+” operator was exclusive—strictly undefined on overlapping classes—because Boole modeled logical addition on ordinary numerical addition, where overlapping elements would be double-counted. In 1864, W. S. Jevons’s Pure Logic introduced inclusive disjunction (X + X = X), the reform that produced what we now call Boolean algebra. Peirce arrived at the same reform independently shortly thereafter; Schröder later systematized the framework. When Jevons proposed the inclusive reading directly to Boole in their 1863 correspondence, Boole rejected it and broke off contact rather than accept it—the “Boolean algebra” universally taught today is Jevons’s reformed version, the one Boole himself could not see.

Ajax frames the Union Principle itself as doing the counterpart philosophical modernization on the physical timeline ontology side that Jevons did on the logical side—the same kind of shift from selective/summative operations to coalescent ones, applied to a different layer of reality. What the historical parallel is diagnostic of is not any prediction about how the Union Principle will be received in the future (that would be mind-reading a reception that has not yet happened), but rather the length of time the analogous logical reform took even once its conditions were ripe: from Boole’s 1847 formulation through Jevons’s 1864 reform to widespread acceptance took decades at minimum, with the original formalist actively refusing the coalescent move. The physical-ontology counterpart had to wait another 160 years for the Union Principle to even be stated. The delay is the diagnostic, and what it diagnoses is the sheer magnitude of the conceptual shift involved in moving from summative to coalescent semantics in any domain where summation is the cognitive default—a shift so non-obvious that it can take generations to land even when all the technical pieces are in place.

249

Canonical Table of the Union Principle

AethicMathematicalLogicalDefinition
Late 2025 (definitive formal expression)

Table 3 of the main paper—the formal expression that took Ajax from late 2024 (when the principle was first isolated and named) to late 2025 to achieve. Four rows mapping logical operations to physical manifestations and mathematical form: (1) Logical OR → Physical AND, agreeing superposition, ∪ᵢAᵢ; (2) Logical AND quantum → Physical AND, agreeing superposition, ∪ᵢAᵢ; (3) Logical AND classical → Physical AND, logical intersection, ∩ᵢAᵢ; (4) Classical “OR” → Physical AND, disagreeing superposition, ∪ᵢ(Aᵢ ∩ ⋂ⱼ≠ᵢ¬Aⱼ). The rows reveal that three of the four logical distinctions all manifest as physical AND, differing only in algebraic structure. That three-out-of-four collapse is the structural payload of the entire Union Principle in compressed form.

The table also reveals that classical physics accidentally flattened the distinction between logical and physical domains into a single monolithic AND, obscuring the possibility of the agreeing-superposition category entirely. What the canonical table makes visible is that "physical AND" is not a single operation but three structurally distinct ones (agreeing superposition, logical intersection, and disagreeing superposition), and that classical reasoning collapsed all three into one because it never had occasion to distinguish them. The table is the crystallized output of the Union Principle—the definitive formal expression that the principle had been building toward since 2022, finally achieved in late 2025.

250

Physical AND vs Logical AND Category Separation

AethicOntologicalLogicalConcept
§2.2.5 of main paper

The core move of the Union Principle: physical AND and logical AND are not counterparts—they occupy entirely separate conceptual categories. Logical operations (AND, OR, negation) are structural distinctions governing how information combines algebraically. Physical AND is merely the phenomenological fact of occupation in the block universe. Classical physics imposed a category error by conflating structural operations with phenomenological presence, which is what made agreeing superpositions appear ad hoc rather than structurally natural. Once the categories are separated, three of the four canonical-table rows collapse onto physical AND cleanly.

251

No Elementary Physical OR

AethicOntologicalLogicalConcept
Corollary of the canonical table

A direct corollary: there is no such thing as elementary physical OR. The very notion of physical AND versus physical OR is the category error that classical physics imposed by conflating structural with phenomenological operations. All apparent “physical OR” in reality is disagreeing superposition—nested union-with-negation that manifests as physical AND while forbidding interaction between its components. This is the ontic root behind why an Aethic superposition is at once a logical OR (in algebraic structure) but manifests as physical occupation (what we intuitively call AND).

252

Two Pathways to Physical AND

AethicLogicalMathematicalConcept
§2.2.5 of main paper

Two structurally distinct pathways yield the same physical result. Additive pathway: adding information to an Aethus instills a logical conjunction (Aethic intersection), directly asserting that multiple states coexist. Subtractive pathway: subtracting information from an Aethus instills a logical disjunction (Aethic union), creating a conceptual blank that becomes a superposition by the Second Postulate. Either pathway results in both possible options physically occurring at once in the block universe—physical AND. This is the heart of the Union Principle’s power: quantum “and” can be re-rendered as logical “or” via agreeing superposition while still replicating the empirical effect of coexisting scenarios by means of its physical AND.

253

Agreeing Superposition Formal Structure

AethicMathematicalDefinition
Equation (1) of main paper Union Principle section

C₁ = ∪ᵢ Aᵢ. A simple elementary union. Represents states that can interfere—the quantum “both slits at once.” Corresponds to quantum coherence: the electron genuinely traverses both paths, and the paths interact to produce an interference pattern. The states coexist and can interfere because they do not mutually forbid each other’s realization. Distinguished from logical intersection not by appearance (both yield physical AND) but by algebraic route—the agreeing superposition is generated by subtractive traversal of the Aethic tree, not additive.

254

Disagreeing Superposition Formal Structure

AethicMathematicalDefinition
Equation (2) of main paper Union Principle section

C₂ = ∪ᵢ(Aᵢ ∩ ⋂ⱼ≠ᵢ ¬Aⱼ). Each state Aᵢ exists together with the explicit negation of every other state. The outer union ensures both “sign-left” and “sign-right” are physically present (physical AND); the inner intersections with negations enforce observational mutual exclusivity (appearing as classical “OR”). When you observe the sign on the left side of the road, your block universe becomes permanently unable to interact with the version where the sign is on the right—not because the right-side version doesn’t exist, but because within your Aethus, that state is explicitly encoded as nonexistent. Precisely matches the empiricism of a timeline-branching model while being ontologically distinct from it.

255

Hidden Hierarchy / Ladder Ontology Extension

AethicOntologicalConcept
§2.2.5 of main paper

The disagreeing superposition effectively creates a hidden hierarchy—a “ladder ontology”—in which more information can be packed into the same physical space than classical physics recognized. Both unions and intersections manifest identically as physical AND, yet their algebraic nesting preserves crucial logical distinctions while maintaining a single phenomenological degree of freedom. Classical physics accidentally flattened this rich structure by treating all physical occupation as monolithic, thereby obscuring the possibility of multiple nested levels operating simultaneously. The ladder is what lets Aethic reasoning carry more structure than the classical ontology can encode without adding extra phenomenological degrees of freedom.

256

Aethic Contradictions vs Aethic Superpositions Rigorously Distinguished

AethicLogicalOntologicalConcept
§2.2.5 of main paper

A fundamental consequence of the Union Principle: we can rigorously distinguish contradictions from superpositions. An Aethic contradiction is what happens when we take the Aethic intersection of two disjoint Aethae. An Aethic superposition is what happens when we take the Aethic union of two Aethae, disjoint or not. Contradictions still exist in Aethic reasoning; they follow from encoding conflicting states directly within the underlying Aethic mathematical structure. Superpositions are indirectly generated by withdrawing states from the underlying structure, tracing out the backward Aethic progression from typical additive knowledge-gain. The interplay between superpositions and the lack of information amounts to the Second Postulate itself.

257

Principle 17: Deepest Layer of the Union Principle

AethicOntologicalPhilosophicalPrinciple
§2.2.5 of main paper—Principle 17

Ajax’s explicit statement of what he considers “the central ontological-categorical core” of algorithmic Aethic reasoning. Even stated-attributes that are disjoint in the sense of Aethic intersection are only emergently—not intrinsically—unable to occupy co-realization in an agreeing superposition. The apparent disjointness arises from invalidity pruning by the environmental context of the given Aethus, not from an intrinsic feature of the attribute itself. Take something classical metaphysics takes for granted as disjoint—“whether this apple is on the right or the left side of the table”—and the Union Principle reveals that its disjointness is environmental, not structural. Physical disjointness remains intrinsic in the sense of Aethic intersections, but there is a second avenue to physical jointness via agreeing superposition, and which of the two applies is contextually decided by the invalidity-pruning algorithms of Aethic reasoning. Ajax footnotes this explicitly as a major contributor to his ranking of the Union Principle as “the single deepest ontological idea of my life so far.”

258

Attribute Categories Resistant to Decomposition

AethicLogicalPhilosophicalConcept
§2.2.5 footnote of main paper

Some categories of Aethic attributes are inherently resistant to investigative decomposition under the Union Principle. A person’s first and last name is the clean example: even defining an Aethus in which environmental ruin of the agreeing superposition can be extracted is invalid by Aethic contradiction, because the attribute of a person’s name already assumes as a definitional component that the information must be transferable—and that assumed transferability invalidates any agreeing superposition by the Third Postulate. By contrast, physical quantities like position or momentum are more manageable, and quantum mechanics already empirically confirms that agreeing superpositions exist for them. The distinction opens what Ajax flags as potentially “an entire investigative philosophical literature at the intersection of semantics, realism, and ontology.”

259

Pre-Linguistic Synesthesia of the Union Principle

AethicMetaEmpiricalPhilosophicalConcept
Late 2022 – early 2023 (Mach-Zehnder animation period)

Ajax’s phenomenological account that the Union Principle hit him as pre-linguistic synesthesia of a concentration bordering on external sensory perception. He reports being able to feel the rhythm of the Aethic alternative in his body as the reason he was able to detach from the standard multiverse view. Developed during months of staring repeatedly at a Mach-Zehnder interferometer animation. Ajax flags this as unique among his ideas: “no other thoughts in my life have had quite the sensory potency as this one.” Primary phenomenological evidence for the claim that the Union Principle is accessible through perceptual engagement rather than propositional reasoning alone.

260

Boolean Logicians Already Intuit the Seed

AethicPhilosophicalConcept
§2.2.6 of main paper

Anyone who has studied Boolean logic has essentially already intuited the basic methodological seed of the Union Principle—they just haven’t recognized that the ontology of possible worlds hasn’t undergone its leg of the same revolution Jevons brought to logic. Consider: when a Boolean logician writes C = A ∨ B, the state of both A and B feeding in “true” does not imply falsehood in C as exclusive OR would. The Boolean logician interprets logical disjunction as catalyzed by agreement rather than inhibited by it. Even in programming, no one carries the timeline-analogous assumption that C must preferentially select one of A or B—it simply acts as their logical disjunctive coalescence. The seed is already present in every Boolean logician’s operational intuition; what the Union Principle does is port it from logic to physical ontology.

261

Disagreeing Superposition ≠ Exclusive OR

AethicLogicalConcept
§2.2.6 footnote 28 of main paper

Although superficially similar, a disagreeing superposition and an exclusive OR are categorically different. A disagreeing superposition retains the Aethic structural distinction between the forward and backward Aethic tree directions; the exclusive OR collapses this distinction ad hoc. Under function composition the two behave entirely differently: iterated XORs reinstate intersections after multiple levels, while disagreeing superpositions insist on keeping the given states locked off from one another across any number of additional nesting levels. In the logical-metaphysical sense, the exclusive OR is an ad hoc human construction which reaches for the disagreeing-superposition concept while remaining structurally irrelevant to it. The underlying culprit, as throughout the Union Principle story, is the conflation in sentential logic between agreeing superpositions and logical intersections due to the classical formalism having flattened the Aethic structural differences in the first place.

The specific diagnostic example Ajax gives in footnote 29 of §2.2.6, reproduced verbatim:

“As another illustration of their differences, note how function composition behaves entirely differently under each, where exclusive ors reinstate intersections after multiple levels, (as in, A ⊕ (B ⊕ C) as a set contains A ∩ B ∩ C as a subset, whereas disagreeing superpositions of states forbid all Aethic intersections between those states—in the sense that a disagreeing superposition is always a disjoint Aethus to the Aethic intersection of any subset of the states involved), whereas disagreeing superpositions insist on keeping the given states locked off from one another given any number of additional levels.”

Footnote 30 of the same section follows up with the corollary: one might be tempted to reason that if disagreeing superposition is like XOR, then because the residue of ordinary disjunction from XOR is the intersection, the intersection must then be the agreeing superposition. Ajax’s Aethic refutation of this chain is twofold—both that agreeing superpositions are not intersections and that disagreeing superpositions are not exclusive ORs. This distinction is not asserted so much as referenced as a corollary of the deeper Aethic structure: the whole XOR-like intuition dissolves as soon as one takes the Union Principle’s logical-physical decoupling seriously. The practical upshot is that readers trained in sentential logic must actively unlearn the intuition that disagreeing-superposition structure is “covered” by the XOR concept already in their toolkit—it is not, and the A ⊕ (B ⊕ C) ⊇ A ∩ B ∩ C example is the cleanest counterexample Ajax offers for why the replacement is required rather than a pedagogical preference.

262

Agreeing Superposition ≠ Logical Intersection

AethicLogicalConcept
§2.2.6 of main paper

The refinement Aethic structure makes visible that Boolean logic does not: an agreeing superposition of A and B is not equivalent to the intersection A ∧ B. Agreeing superpositions force their common attribute’s encoding to be blank; intersections force both attributes into the Aethus—traversing opposite directions on the Aethic tree while still rendering phenomenologically as “both” on the side of physical AND. The confusion between the two is “a leading culprit for why the Aethic Union Principle is only now being named”—it is merely a hiccup in the standard formalism which flattened a physically meaningful distinction. Understanding this non-equivalence is what allows Aethic logic to supersede classical logic.

263

Forward vs Backward Aethic Tree Directions

AethicOntologicalMathematicalConcept
Consequence of Aethic mathematical structure

The Aethic tree has two structurally distinct directions. Forward traversal adds attributes, moving toward richer Aethae—this is the direction of classical knowledge-gain and produces logical intersections. Backward traversal subtracts attributes, moving toward sparser Aethae—this is what generates conceptual blanks, which the Second Postulate then materializes as agreeing superpositions. Although both forward and backward operations can yield the same phenomenological physical AND, they are algebraically opposite and traverse opposite directions on the underlying tree. Much of the Union Principle’s power comes from recognizing that these two routes exist and are genuinely distinct even when empirically indistinguishable at the phenomenological level.

264

Ontic Choppiness in Timeline Metaphysics

AethicMetaPhilosophicalConcept
§2.2.6 title of main paper

The diagnostic term §2.2.6 uses for the specific conceptual defect the Union Principle resolves on the physical-ontology side. “Choppiness” names the feature of classical timeline metaphysics in which operations are implicitly required to be selective or summative rather than coalescent—operations must yield single definite outputs, which forecloses the possibility of operations that produce coalesced results. Just as Boole’s pre-Jevons algebra was choppy in the logical domain (disjunction strictly undefined on overlapping classes), the timeline metaphysics that classical physics has lived with since the 19th century is choppy in the physical-ontology domain (two “paths” cannot be co-occupied without reducing to one). The Union Principle is the de-choppying move, applied to the physical layer.

446

Interior-vs-Exterior Attribution of Non-Interactivity

AethicPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §2.2.6 footnote

A diagnostic observation from a footnote in §2.2.6 about where the non-interactivity between the pockets of a disagreeing superposition is attributed—and why the Union Principle’s answer to that question is what lets it stay consistent with the Boolean revolution in sentential logic. Empirically we know that non-interaction is enforced between the relative systems in a disagreeing superposition, so the structural source of that enforcement has to come from somewhere. The classical multiverse framework assumes the source must be the exterior operation that holds the states apart—the “or” which combines the pockets. This move forces the classical “or” to function precisely the way intuition suggests it does in classical metaphysics: with strict one-or-the-other choosiness that rules out any coalescent semantics.

The fallacious downstream consequence of this choice, as Ajax argues in footnote form, is that it directly generates the choppiness problem articulated in §2.2.6 (see #264 Ontic Choppiness in Timeline Metaphysics): the nature of physical-world “ors” is artificially deemed inequivalent to the precedent already established by sentential logic for how a disjunction ought to operate. The civilizational commitment to Boolean sentential logic is deep enough to find this inequivalence suspicious—so, tracing the problem back to the source, the Union Principle proposes an alternative attribution: the non-interactivity comes from the interior of the pockets, not the exterior disjunction. Each pocket internally states, via conjunctive logical negations, that the other pockets are logically negated. It is not that the pockets are externally blocked off from one another; it is that each one internally explicitly forbids the others by nested logical structure, exactly as the formal statement of the inner nesting specifies.

The consequences of this interior attribution are substantial. First, it leaves the outer Aethic disjunction as a clean disjunction consistent with Boole–Jevons inclusive “or”—no special metaphysical choosiness is required of it. Second, it predicts by process of elimination the existence of agreeing superpositions as the dual case: if the non-interactivity comes from internal nested negations, then whenever a pocket fails to internally forbid the others, whatever it ceases to forbid automatically registers as an agreeing superposition where interaction is permitted. This is the precise payoff the Union Principle extracts from the interior attribution, and it is why Ajax frames the deepest layer of the Union Principle (see #257) as a consequence rather than a further axiom: once the non-interactivity is interiorized, the deepest layer and the agreeing-superposition prediction both fall out, rather than having to be separately imposed. A compact structural diagnosis that distinguishes the Union Principle’s conceptual move from the classical multiverse framework at the level of where in the formal apparatus the non-interactivity lives—not what it is.

447

FTC Suspension of Disbelief (Accordance vs Drake)

NexicMethodologicalConcept
Nexic paper footnote 9, §1.3.4

The methodological analogy Ajax deploys in footnote 9 of the Nexic paper to explain why the Accordance Principle approach to natural history is structurally superior to the Drake equation, rather than just being a different formalism. The claim is that the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus licenses a step of “suspension of disbelief” in which we stop trying to compute the area under a curve directly (as Archimedes would have, via tangible geometric construction) and instead look at an antiderivative F(x) whose difference just happens to produce the same numerical result via a completely different operation. The antiderivative is one full layer of abstraction removed from the geometric content it is measuring, yet it is far more powerful than direct construction because it lets the structural symmetry of the problem do the work that concrete inference cannot. The exact language of footnote 9:

For a methodological analogy, this is somewhat like how the fundamental theorem of calculus, by establishing the integral as the antiderivative, effectively removes us that much from concrete inference. Rather than having to tangibly construct geometry like Archimedes’s proof of the volume of a sphere, we form a step of “suspension of disbelief” where a function f(x) is abstractly reinterpreted as the derivative of a given function F(x). That is, we are not actually carrying out the area under the curve of f(x) directly, but instead are looking at an entirely different object F(x) which just so happens to, while doing a different thing (i.e. difference rather than area difference) produce an equivalent result.

Ajax frames this as the same structural move the Accordance Principle is making relative to the Drake equation. The Drake equation is the Archimedean path: it tries to directly infer the human-hospitality of a natural history from the explicit attribute content of that history, positing multiplied terms (rate of star formation, fraction with planets, fraction habitable, etc.) and reading the result off the product. The Accordance Principle is the antiderivative path: it sidesteps direct attribute-content inference entirely and instead uses a single deductive mediation via Aethic mediocrity, arriving at the same kind of probabilistic conclusions through a route that is one layer of abstraction removed from the underlying generating phenomena. The “suspension of disbelief” consists in accepting that you are no longer reasoning about the concrete generating mechanisms of life on exoplanets—you are reasoning about an abstract structural property (the mediocrity of one’s own Aethus among Aetherian Aethae) that happens to encode the same information through the mediocrity theorem in the same way the antiderivative encodes the area.

Footnote 56 of the same paper extends the diagnosis with a harsher follow-up analogy. “Where the Accordance Principle tries to emulate the dynamical minimalism of Newton’s second law,” Ajax writes, “the Drake equation would be like describing all of physics with polynomial interpolation over position instead, with Runge’s phenomenon remaining intact and all.” The positive reason is the same as the FTC case: the Drake equation “embraces multitudes of assumptions for the sake of specificity,” while the Accordance Principle “embraces an almost bitter degree of minimalism in assumptions, in the tradition of Newton’s hypotheses non fingo.” The self-undercutting failure mode of Drake is that natural history is so immensely complicated that a handful of multiplied terms undershoots that complexity regardless—so the sacrifice of minimalism does not even buy the specificity it claims to. The FTC analogy provides the positive case: a move that looks like cheating because it is not computing the target quantity directly can be structurally sound when the underlying theorem guarantees the equivalence.

Worth noting how this analogy does load-bearing work in three downstream places in Nexic reasoning. First, it motivates the Indiscriminate Time Principle’s treatment of the Drake equation as ill-defined (#189): the Drake equation is not just less powerful than the Accordance Principle—it is structurally committed to a sequential-model ontology that the Aethic framework has already replaced, and the FTC analogy explains why this is not a bug that could be patched but a consequence of the inferential style. Second, it motivates the Open Cosmos (#76) as an abstract medium for reasoning about alternate histories: engaging with the Open Cosmos is itself a suspension-of-disbelief move where the reasoner treats Proxima Centauri b as an alternate history of Earth rather than as a physically separate object (see #449). Third, it licenses the entire Optimal-Earth Conjecture procedure, which performs abstract perturbations over Aethic weights rather than concrete counterfactual construction—another instance of the same antiderivative-style abstract structural move. The FTC footnote is therefore doing more work than its placement as a single footnote in §1.3.4 suggests; it is articulating the methodological stance that licenses the framework’s most characteristic moves.

448

IMD Unification of Destructive and Prior-Lowering Events

NexicLogicalConcept
Nexic paper §A.3

The central unifying claim of the Imperfection Magnitude Drop section (§A.3 of the Nexic paper): two scenarios that look categorically different under classical reasoning—a Hadean gamma-ray burst that directly destroys Earth’s habitability, and a red-dwarf planetary setup that merely severely reduces the prior Bayesian probability of habitability ever arising—turn out to be the same kind of thing under Nexic analysis. Under classical reasoning, the distinction feels load-bearing because one is a catastrophic event that happens in time and the other is a standing condition of the environment that was always there. The Nexic claim is that this distinction is arbitrary: what matters is only the quantity P(H ∩ A_i), the intersection probability of the scenario with Aetherian existence under the Cosmic Nexus. A gamma-ray burst and a red-dwarf habitability ceiling can produce the same numerical value for that intersection, and when they do, they are the same kind of devastation. The exact formulation from §A.3:

A major intrigue of IMDs, as per this principle, is that they unify absurd nightmare-scenarios like the Hadean gamma-ray burst scenario with the (seemingly) more hospitable scenarios highlighted as probabilistically futile by the High-Contrast Earth Hypothesis, (like the classic red dwarf-Aetherian scenario). Rather than having to arbitrarily distinguish between these ill-fated scenarios, in Nexic reasoning they may be regarded as different locations along a spectrum of IMDs by probabilistic severity. So, to be clear, although it might intuitively appear that the Hadean gamma-ray scenario and the red dwarf-Aetherian scenario are categorically separate—that is because the former is a direct fatal destruction of Earth’s habitability whereas the latter merely severely limits the prior Bayesian probability of Earth becoming habitable to begin with—the IMD-based unification highlights this intuition as arbitrary. In both cases, the intersection probability respecting to the Cosmic Nexus of an Aetherian and the Aethus defining the scenario is minute enough to be an IMD, and that is the only objective signifier of devastation without straying into arbitrary convention.

The unification is load-bearing for the Optimal-Earth Conjecture because it lets Ajax reason about the entire space of perturbations away from our natural history on a single probabilistic spectrum rather than having to maintain two separate ontological categories (“disasters” versus “unlikely histories”). Once the unification is in place, the counting argument in the same section—that the number of descriptively articulable Aethae increases cascadingly as probability decreases, so that the local neighborhood of any “named natural history” is dominated by IMD cases—applies uniformly across both types. Without the unification, the counting argument would have to be repeated separately for catastrophes and for simple improbabilities, and the combined result would be harder to articulate and would lose the one-dimensional-spectrum character that gives the argument its force.

The deeper methodological point is that the “direct destructive versus prior-lowering” distinction is the kind of ontological category that Nexic reasoning tries to dissolve in general. Classical reasoning treats them separately because classical reasoning presumes a sequential-model ontology in which events happen on a pre-existing timeline and are evaluated for their causal effects on it. Under the Aethic ontology, there is no pre-existing timeline for events to cause effects on—there is only the space of Aethae and their relative weights under various Nexae. In that space, the only question is which Aethae have low weight relative to the Cosmic Nexus and simultaneously high weight relative to the Aetherian Nexus, which is exactly the IMD criterion. The gamma-ray-burst Aethus and the red-dwarf Aethus are just two coordinates in the same Aethus-space, and the distinction between “direct destruction” and “prior-lowering” turns out to be a feature of how classical reasoning imposes sequential narrative on them rather than a feature of the Aethae themselves.

This is also one of the clearest downstream instances of the “barrier between any two things is an illusion” pattern (#186) showing up in mature Nexic reasoning: what looked like an ontological wall between two distinct kinds of improbability turns out to be an artifact of coarse classical categorization. Ajax also flags the unification as directly relevant to his methodological move of reasoning about “hypothetical disasters” and “alternate histories” under a single apparatus, which in turn licenses the Vulcan / Proxima b alternate-history treatment (#449) without having to smuggle in a separate theory of what counts as a disaster versus a simple variation.

449

Vulcan & Proxima Centauri as Alternate Histories of Earth

NexicThought ExperimentConcept
Nexic paper §2.4; Fermi Paradox video (Oct 2023)

Ajax’s dual illustration of the Aethic Alternate History Theorem—that any planet in the Open Cosmos, whether physically existent (Proxima Centauri b) or purely fictional (Vulcan from Star Trek), can be legitimately treated as an alternate history of Earth rather than as a spatially separate object. The move is one of the most methodologically strange-looking in Nexic reasoning because it dissolves the intuitive distinction between “a planet far away in space” and “a hypothetical Earth with different parameters,” but it falls out directly from two facts about the Open Cosmos: every event one has learned about has an Aethus in which it could have held the alternative outcome, and the Aethic tree lets us reach arbitrarily distant configurations by walking up through parent Aethae and then back down through child Aethae.

The Proxima Centauri b passage from §2.4 of the Nexic paper:

It should also be noted that perhaps the central motivation of the Open Cosmos concept is that it allows us to treat any planet in our universe, be it already existent, fictional, or hypothetical outright, as being representable as an “alternate history” of the Earth itself. This, therefore, triggers something of a major reframing in which all possible scenarios which concern the evolution of life are to be thought of as if we had relocated the planetary system in question to the “canvas” of Earth, and all reachable alternatives to it on the Aethic tree, (that is, by removing properties on-demand via parent Aethae, and then instating alternatives via child Aethae). So, even a physically existent system like Proxima Centauri b, in Nexic reasoning, is not regarded by its spatial separation from us at all, but instead is modeled by the process of abstractly altering all of the Aethic properties of present Earth, until such a system has been constructed verbatim.

The Vulcan passage from the October 19, 2023 Fermi Paradox video is more informal but makes the structural move more vivid:

A planet is supposed to be the same planet with time. I mean, sure, I get why that’s relevant to our usual endeavors. But hey, why is past Earth called Earth, present Earth called Earth, alternate-history Earth called Earth, but Vulcan called Vulcan? We can trace any sum of properties about Earth up the Aethic tree and then trace them down again until we recreate Vulcan from Star Trek. There’s no dividing line between alternate Earth of one type and alternate Earth of another, because Vulcan is identical to a version of Earth in a quote-unquote alternate history. And still we don’t call it Earth.

Three structural points worth drawing out of the pairing. First, the Vulcan case is methodologically important precisely because Vulcan is fictional—the argument would lose its force if the only examples were real exoplanets. The fictional case shows that “having actually existed in our physical universe” is not doing any work in the alternate-history classification; what does the work is the Aethic path from our current Aethus through parent and child Aethae to the target Aethus. Second, Proxima Centauri b is important for the complementary reason: it shows that the argument is not merely about fictional scenarios—any real exoplanet is equivalently an alternate history of Earth. The two examples together bracket the full range: one purely fictional, one as concretely physical as a foreign planet can be. Third, the argument also applies to past and future Earths by the same mechanism: past Earth is an alternate history to present Earth by exactly the same Aethic-tree walk. The video’s “past Earth called Earth, present Earth called Earth, alternate-history Earth called Earth, but Vulcan called Vulcan” sequence is explicitly staging this equivalence.

This is where the framework’s anti-sequential instinct is doing the load-bearing work. The apparent continuity of “Earth through time” turns out to be a Nexic coincidence of heavy co-occurrence weighting in the Aetherian Nexus, not an ontological fact about planetary identity. A reader who first learns about the Martin Luther counterfactual in §2.3 of the Nexic paper still calls the counterfactual world “Earth” without hesitation; Ajax’s argument is that this intuitive move generalizes all the way out to Vulcan and Proxima b once you stop privileging spatial coordinates. The deeper payoff is that the Accordance Principle can then operate uniformly over this entire space of “alternate Earths”—the Optimal-Earth Conjecture literally treats every Aetherian Aethus as a kind of alternate Earth, whether it corresponds to a real exoplanet, a fictional one, a past-Earth configuration, or a pure hypothetical. The unification buys the framework its quantitative reach.

451

Cosmic Accordance Principle (Principle 3)

NexicPhilosophicalPrinciple
Nexic paper Principle 3, §2.3

Principle 3 of the Nexic paper, stated explicitly in §2.3 immediately after the Strong Aethic Alternate History Theorem (#302) and the Biological Hyperblock (#303) construction. The principle:

If oneself is an Aetherian, then for any subspace of a biological hyperblock cosmos which one is given to occupy, one can see themselves to take the form of a uniformly random Aetherian in the set of all independent Aetherians in that subspace.

The Cosmic Accordance Principle is the spatial-mediocrity form of the Accordance Principle—structurally the same claim as the Anthropic Accordance Principle (#11), but phrased as a mediocrity statement over the biological hyperblock cosmos rather than over the abstract Aethic tree. The derivation runs through the standard mediocrity principle applied to the hyperblock. Since the processes and circumstances of the cosmos that are likelier to generate Aetherians will house a higher density of Aetherians in the hyperblock, a mediocre observer will tend to exist within the circumstances of highest Aetherian density, and therefore will have to exist in the high-probability-of-Aetherian-generation regions of the biological hyperblock. Ajax explicitly frames this as a “reverse engineering” move: from the condition that we are typically present among the highest-likelihood circumstances relative to the Cosmic Nexus, we can reverse-engineer the original probabilities of happenings in the cosmos.

The result is mathematically equivalent to the Anthropic Accordance Principle, with the inequality:

P(H | A′) / P(H | A)  ≲  P(A) / (1 − P(A))

Two structural observations worth drawing out. First, the principle makes the spatial intuition behind anthropic reasoning formal. Under classical anthropic reasoning, “we find ourselves in the special places” is a heuristic without a rigorous scope—we cannot quite say where the “special places” live. But under the biological hyperblock construction, “the special places” is a well-defined geometric region (the high-Aetherian-density subspace of the hyperblock), and a mediocrity statement over that region is a precise mathematical claim rather than a vague instinct. Second, the principle’s equivalence to the Accordance Principle is not a coincidence but a direct consequence of the Strong Aethic Alternate History Theorem: once spatial separation and Aethic-tree separation are structurally interchangeable, any mediocrity statement expressed in one frame translates to the other. The Cosmic Accordance Principle is the spatial-form dual, and the Anthropic Accordance Principle (#11) is the Aethic-form dual, of the same underlying mediocrity commitment. That the two formulations coincide numerically is precisely what one would predict from the Strong Theorem’s equivalence claim, and the coincidence serves as a sanity check for both.

Worth noting that this gives the Accordance Principle an additional derivational route. The Anthropic Accordance Principle can be motivated through the Hațeg Island-style fluke-skepticism argument (#11); the Cosmic Accordance Principle can be motivated through the biological hyperblock spatial-mediocrity argument. Having two independent derivations converging on the same inequality makes the underlying claim more robust. Ajax flags this explicitly in the paper: the Cosmic Accordance Principle “represents another means of deriving” the Accordance Principle, and the two paths share only the mediocrity commitment at the axiomatic level.

452

Aethic Reformulation of the Tensor Product

AethicMathematicalOntologicalConcept
Aethic paper §10.2, footnote 215

The claim that the Aethic treatment of composite quantum systems is not a reinterpretation of the tensor product but a full reformulation of it—the tensor product recovered as a special case of the Aethic definitional hierarchy. This is one of the most mathematically ambitious single claims in the Aethic paper, and Ajax frags it with an unusual “I must emphasize” in the prose, marking it as something he wants the reader to notice as a structural commitment rather than a passing comment. The exact statement from §10.2:

The combined effect of this Aethic reframe, I must emphasize, is a complete reformulation of the tensor product for composite quantum systems in the mathematical language of the Aethic definitional hierarchy. That is, notice how implicit in the example system Aethus we have been describing is an Aethic attribute for the destination point for the particle, for the child system Aethus in Aethic superposition which decides the valid intersected terms as its basis elements, and then the basis elements themselves for valid path subsets within each one of those child system Aethae. In the standard quantum formalism, we would describe precisely this same structure by taking a tensor product between the customized Hilbert spaces of these three Aethic attributes—so the result is equivalent—just as we know.

The three-layered example Ajax walks through in the passage makes the reformulation concrete. Pick a composite quantum system with (i) a destination-point attribute for the particle, (ii) a child-system attribute for the basis elements that decide which terms are intersected, and (iii) the basis elements themselves selecting valid path subsets within each child system Aethus. In the standard formalism these three layers would be combined by taking a tensor product between three customized Hilbert spaces, one per attribute. In the Aethic formalism they are combined by Aethic conjunction within the definitional hierarchy—the destination-point attribute is an Aethic attribute, the child-system attribute is another, the basis elements are a third, and the composite Aethus is formed by conjoining them. The result is numerically equivalent where both formalisms apply, but the Aethic route reaches that result via the definitional hierarchy and Aethic algebra rather than via the Hilbert-space tensor product machinery.

Footnote 215 extends the claim with the dramatic corollary. The full Aethic treatment lets us see the Hilbert space formalism itself as “a profoundly special case of the wider Aethic concepts of universal Aethic superposition, the Aethic structure, and Aethic algebra and pruning.” The generality of the quantum Hilbert space formalism is then “limited only to the tiny interface between the commitment to the cosine kernel found in the Quantum Nexus and the relevant Aethic structure present there, but with the full Aethic reasoning in hand we enable the separation and substantial expansion of both of these prospects in the interface.” The substantive content of the corollary is that the Hilbert-space formalism is doing two things at once—enforcing a particular (cosine) interference kernel via the Quantum Nexus, and imposing an Aethic structure on the set of basis elements—and that standard quantum mechanics couples these two commitments tightly because it has no separate vocabulary for them. The Aethic reformulation provides separate vocabulary for each, which is what lets the framework distinguish between “what would change if we used a different interference kernel” and “what would change if we used a different basis structure” as two independent directions of generalization rather than one entangled direction.

Three notes on what this claim is and is not. First, it is not a displacement of the tensor product. Ajax is emphatic throughout §10 (and in footnote 216, the “diversity of ingredients” cake analogy) that the Lagrangian and the standard quantum machinery are to be preserved and augmented, not contested—the goal is to “bring out the highlights in the Lagrangian,” not to replace it. The tensor product reformulation is a reformulation in the strict sense: the new formulation reproduces the old one as a special case and exposes the structural commitments that the old one was silently enforcing. Second, the claim is load-bearing for the dual-classical-limit result (#371) that follows in §10.2.1. If the tensor product were merely reinterpreted rather than reformulated, the second Aethic mechanism of classical emergence (decoherence as literal path-set restriction, #375) would not have the ontological standing to operate alongside the stationary-action mechanism—it would be a phenomenological add-on rather than a structural feature. The reformulation is what makes the dual-mechanism story coherent. Third, the claim gestures toward a research program: separating the cosine-kernel commitment from the Aethic-structure commitment opens a space of “near-quantum” theories that vary one while holding the other fixed, which is a concrete place where Aethic reasoning could make quantitative predictions beyond the standard Hilbert-space formalism. The paper does not pursue this program, but the reformulation is what makes the program well-defined to pursue.

243

The Inheritance Paradox

AethicLogicalConcept
May 8, 2023 (completing insight)

The recognition that any inheritance statement about the validity of an Aethus must hold not only for the current Aethus but for every possible child Aethus of it. A valid state must remain valid under all possible information updates, not just the current one. This is the specific insight that completed Aethic reasoning on May 8, 2023—Ajax’s realization that “the existing inheritance statement had to be true, but I also noted that it was going to have to be true for not only the observer’s Aethus in the Aethic soccer field, but for every possible one of their child Aethae.” The extension from single-Aethus validity to universally-quantified child validity is what converts the naive invalidation rule into the Third Postulate proper, and it is the specific cognitive step that marked “the instant of completion for Aethic reasoning” per Ajax’s own account.

244

Agreeing Superposition as Substrate

AethicOntologicalLogicalConcept
Consequence of Postulates 2 and 3 operating in concert

A structural observation often missed in presentations of the Third Postulate: the Third Postulate operates over agreeing superpositions as its domain, not upon them as its output. Agreeing superposition is what the Second Postulate produces by default for anything Aethically unknown; the Third Postulate prunes agreeing superpositions that would lead to contradictions, forcing the system into disagreeing superposition where checkmate holds. This means the Second and Third Postulates have a productive–retractive relationship: the Second generates, the Third selectively removes, and the combined effect is the empirical Aethic state. Understanding this substrate relationship is essential for seeing why the postulates are not redundant and why neither alone would suffice—the Second without the Third would produce agreeing superposition universally, and the Third without the Second would have nothing to prune.

245

Two-Generational Look-Ahead (∃B⊂A, ∀C⊂B)

AethicLogicalMathematicalConcept
Formalized in Third Postulate final form

The specific quantifier structure that makes the Third Postulate work: V[A] ⇒ ∃B ⊂ A, ∀C ⊂ B, V[C]. Validity of A requires an existential child B of A such that every grandchild C of B is itself valid. The two-generational structure distinguishes check (where a threat exists but can be escaped) from checkmate (where every possible escape still contains the threat). The quantifier alternation ∃∀ is essential—a single-generational version would be too weak to capture the dynamic pruning the framework requires, and a naive version without the two-generational look-ahead would fail to reproduce the double-slit interference pattern correctly. The chess analogy Ajax uses throughout is precise at this structural level: check is a single-generation threat, checkmate is the two-generation closure, and the Third Postulate operationalizes the closure as an Aethic pruning rule.

246

The Valid Escape Structure

AethicLogicalConcept
Formalized in Third Postulate final form

The Third Postulate’s formulation ensures that a state is invalid only when every possible move leads to a position that is still in check. Valid states are those where at least one escape path exists, not those free of current threat. This distinction is what makes the postulate operational: it does not forbid states with reachable contradictions in some branches, only states where all branches reach contradictions. Preserves the framework’s ability to handle check-like situations without over-generating invalidity. Also the reason the postulate can be expressed analytically (as a single ∃∀ condition) rather than piecewise (as a list of cases)—the valid escape structure is what collapses what would otherwise be a case-by-case enumeration into a single quantifier expression.

247

Mere-Possibility Checkmate

AethicLogicalPhilosophicalConcept
August 22, 2022, 1:42 PM journal entry

The insight Ajax articulated at 1:42 PM on August 22, 2022: the mere structural possibility of asking a question that would produce a contradiction is enough to invalidate the interference pattern. The contradiction does not need to be actually realized—only structurally available. This is the moment in the derivation where the naive framing “invalidation requires actual measurement” becomes “invalidation follows from the structural availability of measurement,” a cognitive shift that opened the way to the Third Postulate’s final form. Without this insight, the postulate would be triggered only by actual observations and would fail to derive wave-function collapse in the general case. The “Aw crap” moment of the soccer field journal entry is the primary-source record of this exact cognitive shift, documented in real time across the 19-minute gap between the 1:23 PM and 1:42 PM timestamps.

44

Aethic State

AethicDefinitionalDefinition
§3.2.2 of main paper

The lowest layer of the definitional hierarchy of Aethic reasoning: a single piece of raw data (e.g., the integer “8”), an instance drawn from the class of all states an Aethic attribute might take. Analogous to a value in a key-value pair, where the key is the attribute (the question, e.g. “what is Tim’s favorite ice cream flavor?”) and the state is one specific possible answer to that question.

Foundational because the entire layered ontology of Aethic reasoning—state, attribute, expression, Aethus—is built up by composition starting from the state primitive. States are what get “stated” when an agreeing superposition resolves into a definite value during an Aethic update; they are also what populate the operational machinery of the framework (union, intersection, partition, equivalence, weighting, subtraction). Distinct from possible-worlds atoms in modal realism: a state is one specific value of one specific attribute, not a complete specification of one entire possible reality. This finer-grained primitive is what lets Aethic reasoning be type-theoretic rather than set-theoretic, and what allows the framework to compose Aethae out of building blocks small enough to support the union, intersection, and validity machinery the later postulates depend on.

45

Aethic Attribute

AethicDefinitionalDefinition
§3.2.3 of main paper

A class of possible states equipped with an identifier and a set of relations to other attributes. Analogous to a question—e.g., “What is Tim’s favorite ice cream flavor?”—with each possible flavor being a state the attribute could take.

46

Aethic Stated-Attribute

AethicDefinitionalDefinition
§3.2.4 of main paper

An attribute instantiated with a particular state—analogous to an answer. This is the 1-Aethus, the fundamental unit of fact in the framework.

47

Static Aethus

AethicDefinitionalDefinition
§3.2.5 of main paper

A specific set of stated-attributes describing a system. Viewed as a function mapping attributes to states, this is the 2-Aethus; extended to the 3-Aethus by closure under all derivable attributes (logical completion). Many different static Aethae can represent the same knowledge state—their equivalence class under Aethic equivalence is the full Aethus (the 4-Aethus).

48

Aethic Equivalence

AethicMathematicalDefinition
§3.2.6 of main paper

Two static Aethae are Aethically equivalent if they are fully derivable from one another—that is, if neither contains information the other cannot recover. The Aethus itself is defined as an equivalence class of static Aethae under this relation, bridging the gap between concrete information and the actual ontological nature of the system.

49

Blank Aethic Attributes

AethicMathematicalDefinitionalDefinition
§3.3.1 of main paper

Attributes not present in a 3-Aethus, representing unknowing. Including or excluding blank stated-attributes always generates the same Aethus (Aethic equivalence), creating an automatic power-set-like layer of equivalence. The blank state is a genuinely new logical primitive—neither true nor false, but ontologically productive (retrieving all states via the Second Postulate). Structurally different from fuzzy logic or intuitionistic logic, and perhaps the first example of a non-classical logical value with direct empirical predictions.

50

Aethic Contradiction

AethicLogicalMathematicalDefinition
§3.3.3 of main paper

A physically unrealizable informational state containing direct conflicting information. Distinguished sharply from superposition: contradictions arise from excessive conflicting information; superpositions arise from the lack of information. This distinction—made possible by the definitional hierarchy—is the foundation of the framework’s ability to treat uncertainty and impossibility as different phenomena.

51

Invalid Aethae

AethicLogicalMathematicalDefinition
§3.3.3 of main paper

Aethae containing one or more Aethic contradictions, and therefore physically unrealizable. Invalidity is the category the Third Postulate prunes: if every proper child Aethus of some Aethus is invalid, then that Aethus itself is invalid. Invalid Aethae carry zero weight in the weighted ring of Aethae and thus contribute nothing to probability.

52

Aethic Union Operation

AethicMathematicalDefinition
§3.3.7 of main paper

Corresponds to probabilistic event union—removes conflicting information to produce superposition. In the parent/child direction of the Aethic tree, union moves up toward parent Aethae (less specified). Deliberately mirrors probability unions rather than set-theoretic unions: the contrast principle is that Aethic unions subtract information while intersections add it.

53

Aethic Intersection Operation

AethicMathematicalDefinition
§3.3.7 of main paper

Corresponds to probabilistic event intersection—combines information, potentially producing contradictions if inconsistent. In the parent/child direction of the Aethic tree, intersection moves down toward child Aethae (more specified). Together with union, it forms the pair of operations on which the commutative ring structure of weighted Aethae is built.

54

Weighted Aethae (Aethic Weighting)

AethicMathematicalConcept
§4 of main paper

Extends the strict present/blank dichotomy to allow real-valued linear combinations of Aethae—“Aethic weights” representing partial attribute presence and corresponding to probabilistic quantities. This generalization is what connects Aethic reasoning to standard probability theory and allows dynamic, rather than binary, handling of uncertainty.

55

Commutative Ring Structure of Weighted Aethae

AethicMathematicalTheorem
§4.4 of main paper

The set of weighted Aethae forms a commutative ring, with Aethic union as addition and Aethic intersection as multiplication. Proven via distributivity, closure, and the existence of additive inverses (with the hyperinvalid Aethus as additive identity). This makes probability a structural feature of the Aethic graph rather than an interpretive overlay—a novel foundation for probability theory unifying subjective and objective perspectives.

56

Aethic Partition

AethicMathematicalDefinition
§3.4.2 of main paper

The Aethic analogue to a set partition—a collection of valid, mutually disjoint Aethae whose union reconstructs the parent Aethus. Provides the basis for one of the two Aethic definitions of probability (the partition-based definition) and enables clean handling of exhaustive case analyses within the framework.

57

The Aethic Partition Theorem

AethicMathematicalTheorem
§4.3.3 of main paper

The union of the valid Aethae in an Aethic partition equals the union of all elements in the partition (since invalid Aethae contribute zero weight). A supporting theorem in the weighted-ring development that confirms the internal consistency of Aethic probability structure under partitioning.

58

Parent/Child Aethic Relations

AethicMathematicalDefinitionalDefinition
§3.4 of main paper

A child Aethus inherits all information from its parent plus additional attributes. The inheritance relation is directed and transitive, creating the DAG structure of the Aethic tree. These relations are what the Third Postulate quantifies over—its “∀B ⊂ A, ∃C ⊂ B” structure refers to proper children and grandchildren of A in the parent/child relation.

59

Reduced Form of an Aethus

AethicMathematicalOntologicalDefinition
§6.3 of main paper

The Aethus with all invalid child Aethae pruned by the Third Postulate. The reduced form has two distinct roles in Aethic reasoning, both of which Ajax flags explicitly in the main paper.

First role—substrate of the Third Postulate. The Third Postulate’s checkmate condition operates not on raw Aethae but on their reduced forms—the Aethae from which all invalid descendants have already been pruned. Without the reduced form there is no formal object on which the checkmate operation can act. In this sense the reduced form is the fundamental mathematical substrate underlying the entire collapse-derivation machinery.

Second role—dissolver of retrocausality. The reduced form of an Aethic intersection is a coupling that does not care about temporal ordering at all, because Aethic time is indiscriminate. This is what resolves the delayed-choice experiment, the delayed-choice quantum eraser, and other apparent retrocausality paradoxes—the reduced form couples past and future without requiring backward causation, dissolving the temporal directionality of the paradox.

60

Aethic Template

AethicMathematicalDefinitionalDefinition
§3.2.6 of main paper

The attribute analogue of an Aethus—a structured collection of attributes (rather than stated-attributes). Templates capture the “shape” of an informational state without committing to specific values, which is useful for comparing Aethae across different agents or contexts.

61

Hyperinvalid Aethus

AethicMathematicalDefinition
§4.4.2 of main paper

The additive identity in the commutative ring of weighted Aethae—the “zero” element. Plays the role of a maximal Aethic contradiction, carrying zero probability weight and serving as the absorbing element for many operations. Analogous to the empty set in set theory, but with its meaning inverted: where the empty set is a neutral collection of nothing, the hyperinvalid Aethus is the extreme failure case of informational coherence.

62

Aethic Multiplication Rule of Probability

AethicMathematicalTheorem
§4.2 of main paper

The standard multiplication rule of probability emerges naturally from the Aethic intersection operation on weighted Aethae. A formal result demonstrating that classical probability theory is recovered as a consequence of the Aethic structure rather than needing to be added as an independent axiom.

63

Permanencible Attributes

AethicMathematicalConcept
§6.3 of main paper

Attributes that are permanently unknowable from a given Aethus’s descendants. Connected to Heisenberg’s uncertainty principle as a special case of a broader Aethic uncertainty principle: permanent unknowability is the condition under which agreeing superposition is stable, and permanencibility is its formal name in Aethic reasoning.

64

Aethically Fixed States

AethicMathematicalLogicalConcept
§9 of main paper

States that appear classically fixed but carry behind-the-scenes disagreeing superposition variation. The classical appearance of determinateness emerges from nested disagreeing-superposition structure that forbids interaction, not from actual metaphysical fixity. This bridges the Aethic framework to the classical world’s appearance of settled determinate facts.

65

Mixed Decompositions

AethicMathematicalConcept
§3.3.5 of main paper

The semiblank case of the Decomposition Trichotomy: an attribute is directly blank (not stated in a static Aethus) while not conceptually blank (because some sub-attributes are known). This is the formal mechanism distinguishing disagreeing superposition from agreeing superposition at the level of the Third Postulate’s checkmate condition.

66

The Generalized Fixed Principle

AethicLogicalPrinciple
Named late 2024

Generalizes fixed-versus-variable states from classical logic to Aethic reasoning. Applications extend into the active reasoning work Ajax began in fall 2023. Current foundational weight is hard to assess because most applications are deferred to the next paper.

67

The Aethic Subtraction Principle

AethicMathematicalPrinciple
Named late 2024 (marked speculative)

A speculative principle about whether Aethic complements can be re-rendered through subtraction in the weighted ring. Ajax explicitly marks it as “one interesting, albeit radical idea we might entertain,” placing it in the exploratory rather than foundational category.

68

The Indiscriminate Arrow of Time Principle

BothOntologicalPrinciple
Corollary of Indiscriminate Time · §9.5 of main paper

The Aethic second law of thermodynamics operates indiscriminately across all points in past and future history rather than only at the present moment. A consequence of the Indiscriminate Time Principle applied to thermodynamics: entropy increase is an Aethic, not a dimensional, phenomenon.

69

The Entropic Principle of Aethic Reasoning

AethicOntologicalPrinciple
February 19, 2023 (phone booth thought experiment) · Principle 33 · §9.12.1 of main paper

A time-reversal operation onto the universe would not cause the universe to backtrack along its ordering of events; it would instead cause an instantaneous reversal of all odd-dimensional quantities (velocities and the like) followed by continued entropic increase. Ajax’s specific example: two friends throwing a football at the moment when the reversal occurs. The football does not follow its trajectory back to the thrower’s hands; it gets a kick of momentum back in the direction from which it came, feels air resistance and other entropic effects as if time were still flowing forward, and everything thereafter proceeds normally. The underlying reason is that the Aethic second law of thermodynamics is rooted to Aethic time rather than Einsteinian time, so a reversal along the Einsteinian axis does nothing to the Aethic-time-indexed entropic arrow.

More intuitively, the principle is a consequence of the ladder ontology from the Union Principle combined with the qualitative facet of the Extrusion Principle. When one reverses their own worldline direction and physically backtracks into their past light cone, they should expect the event of different circumstances appearing than “what one left there last time” to be a contradiction. The Aethic explanation is that spacetime was never rigid in its contents to begin with—different Aethic block universes exist as arbitrarily-different perspectival viewings of the matter and energy within the same single physical spacetime. Backtracking one’s worldline is just one means of shifting between preexisting layers of that ladder ontology. A significant corollary: the grandfather paradox is avoided because the block universe at arrival must be different from the original block universe anyway, as mediated by constant entropic increase relative to the observing agent’s Aethae. The interpretation generalizes to closed timelike curves should they exist at all.

70

The Aethic Second Law of Thermodynamics

AethicMathematicalTheorem
§9 of main paper

Interprets entropy increase as a structural feature of Aethic extrusion: the progression along the Markov chain naturally produces the appearance of entropy growth. Thermodynamic arrows emerge from the directionality of information acquisition rather than from any asymmetry in the underlying physics.

71

The High-Contrast Earth Hypothesis

NexicEmpiricalHypothesis
Developed 2021–2022 · §5.1 of Nexic paper

Small perturbations to Earth’s history randomize the probability of Aetherian existence across many orders of magnitude. When conjoined with the Accordance Principle it forms the Strong Accordance Principle (Tier B, position 17)—the form actually used in most specific derivations. Converts the Accordance Principle from a mild probabilistic tendency into an effectively deterministic constraint.

72

The Dark Era Hypothesis

NexicEmpiricalHypothesis
Seed July 24, 2018 alongside Accordance Principle · developed throughout 2018–2023

The Mesozoic period functioned as an evolutionary crucible in which theropod predation pressure was causally necessary for the development of the mammalian limbic system and, by extension, human cognitive architecture. The oldest applied hypothesis in the research program—Ajax’s original 2018 thinking about dinosaurs being human requisites was already implicitly a Dark Era argument before either the Accordance Principle or the Dark Era had formal names. The two concepts were conceptually one for years, gradually refining into distinct forms.

73

Existential Linkage

NexicMathematicalDefinition
Definition 5 of Nexic paper

For stated-attribute A with complement A′, the existential linkage is L_A = [P(H|A) − P(H|A′)] / [P(H|A) + P(H|A′)]. The sign indicates the direction of correlation with Aetherian existence: L_A > 0 favors observers, L_A < 0 disfavors them, L_A = 0 means statistical independence. The Accordance Principle can be equivalently stated as L_A ≳ 1 − 2 P(A), making existential linkage a natural measure of how strongly an event is correlated with observer existence.

74

Nexic Packaging

NexicMethodologicalConcept
§9 of Nexic paper

The inductive analogue to deductive Nexic analysis: when direct optimization is too complex, identify other examples in natural history of the same general trend and transfer the pattern to the current query. The Hooke’s catenary analogy is apt—just as Hooke inferred a dome’s shape from a hanging cord by symmetry, Nexic packaging infers unknown historical facts from known ones by pattern transfer across Nexae. A genuinely new mode of scientific inference.

75

Orders of Nexic Arguments

NexicMethodologicalMathematicalConcept
§2.5 of Nexic paper

A formal system for measuring how many inductive applications of the Accordance Principle separate a claim from direct empirical evidence. Zero-order arguments are empirical statements; first-order arguments apply the Accordance Principle once; higher-order arguments chain applications. The recursive definition O(X) = min over decompositions of max-over-components-plus-one ensures the minimal order is always used, analogous to how ODEs are classified by their highest derivative. Provides rigorous assessment of inferential distance and epistemic risk for any Nexic prediction.

76

The Open Cosmos

NexicMathematicalConcept
§8.4 of Nexic paper

The abstract medium in which Nexic reasoning operates: the set of all possible Aetherian Aethae— including those corresponding to other planets (real, fictional, or purely hypothetical)—treated as “alternate histories of Earth” constructed by abstractly altering Aethic properties via parent and child Aethae. Dissolves the distinction between “alternate timelines” and “other planets” (they are the same thing in the Open Cosmos). The Cosmic Nexus weights events by their volumetric frequency in the Open Cosmos, making it the probability substrate for all Nexic calculations.

The concept is derived in §2.4 of the Nexic paper via a limiting argument over parent Aethae. Because “those you have yet to meet each exist in disagreeing superposition of occupying all possible human lives,” each walk to a further parent Aethus lets more possible humans back into the superposition. The limiting tendency is a state where all possible conscious observers exist throughout the universe in every way they possibly could, weighted by the Cosmic Nexus in its rawest and most general form. That limiting state is the Open Cosmos. One’s own consciousness will have been inhabited in the Open Cosmos at some point along the receding chain of parent Aethae via centric unfolding, which gives the induction its anchor: each intermediate parent Aethus is a step toward the limiting Open Cosmos, so the postulate of cosmic-mediocrity reduces in the limit to the Open-Cosmos case.

The paper’s Concept 1 gives the operational definition:

Let us consider how one ought to best intuit the concept of the Open Cosmos. Simply put, we might imagine that for every event one has learned about, be it past or present so as to have learned about it, we might imagine the Aethus for which it could have held the alternative outcome, or more specifically the Aethus of probability-accurate disagreeing superposition between its realized outcome and alternative outcome.
