By the estimation of Hinduism, the answer would have to be that a single consciousness—Brahman itself—is viewing through each and every lens, such that our perceived differences in mind sprout directly from looking through different Aethus lenses rather than from being different conscious beings. This also highlights the Eastern concept that the lens itself may be a construct which we could see beyond with so much as a thought but simply never choose to do.

Ajax offers one tentative extension: death, from this initial approach, would itself be “the act of realizing with certainty that the lens never existed in the first place, and being able to engage beyond that picture accordingly.” But he explicitly stops himself there, saying he does not want to say more on the topic of death for now because he has not yet gained enough of a foothold on the empiricism to pull off hypotheses non fingo inductive reasoning again, and he will save any assurances for until he can “comfortably avoid presenting armchair philosophy.” The methodological self-restraint is itself significant—it shows that Ajax is willing to gesture at speculative territory but refuses to commit to it without the inductive bedrock that the rest of the framework relies on. The entry preserves both the question and the self-restraint as load-bearing features of the framework’s intellectual posture.

401

Cartesian Substance Dualism Explicitly Insufficient

AethicPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.1 footnote 182

Ajax’s explicit rejection of Cartesian substance dualism as a match for the Sāṃkhya-style intuition Aethic reasoning is tracking. In footnote 182 he writes that “Cartesian substance dualism doesn’t do any justice to this intuition, which speaks of something far far deeper and more profound.” The deeper thing he has in mind is the point of contact where raising an Aethus out of sensations (his example is the sensation of danger) leaves “a mark of information that is just deep enough to not be manipulatable by Western styles of knowledge,” and in the process reflects in one’s own reality as all the Aethically-mediated consequences like superposition.

The rejection matters because the easy Western move when hearing “dualism” in Aethic reasoning is to assume Cartesian substance dualism is the intended frame. Ajax forecloses that reading explicitly: the intuition behind the Aethus-Reality Dualism Principle is “deeply metaphysical and at once not describable with anything I’ve met in the West outside of my own intuitions.” The Sāṃkhya parallel, not the Cartesian one, is the cross-tradition match. This is consequential for downstream critique because any Aethic-reasoning-as-Cartesian-dualism critique is attacking a position Ajax has explicitly disavowed.

402

Canvas-Not-Overwrite Positioning on Non-Materialism

AethicMetaPhilosophicalMethodologicalCriterion
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 185

Ajax’s specific positioning of Aethic reasoning relative to the broader non-materialist consciousness movement. He shares the view that materialism and physicalism ought not to be the final metaphysical answer, and is in great respect of the movement’s work and “pioneering courage in challenging the current metaphysical paradigm.” His differentiation is methodological, not substantive: he worries that the movement’s primary mode of phrasing its claims in standard psychological trial format is not proactive enough to gain a consistent foothold to the degree of creating a new paradigm outright. The best case scenario, he thinks, is that there exists a Bell’s-theorem-like underlying causality to be had, and surface-level correlations would not tap into this any more than GHZ could have been reached by randomly selecting variables.

His positioning is explicit: “I am not advocating that my work ought to overwrite theirs, but rather that I hope my work fills a needed niche in the rigorous establishment of their new paradigm, so as to reinforce rather than overwrite their studies accordingly.” He wants to provide “the canvas upon which their art can be expressed”—the mathematical grounding of the stage that ought to replace materialism, while the movement’s empirical work happens on that stage. The framing is methodologically generous: he treats other non-materialist programs as complementary rather than competitive, and explicitly names the niche Aethic reasoning is trying to fill without claiming precedence over other work in the same broader space.

403

Accordance Principle as First Distinctly Aethic Nonclassicality

BothPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.1 footnote 178

A specific historical-methodological claim Ajax makes in footnote 178: the first distinctly Aethic nonclassicality to show up in his work was the Accordance Principle itself from Nexic reasoning, not the quantum material. The reason is that the mere prospect of the Accordance Principle forces a hard reassessment of what a human actually is, categorically, because of the sheer expansion it provides to the reference class across space and time which a consciousness could reasonably occupy. This is “perhaps the primary reason why the Accordance Principle is so evasive to scientific discovery”—the state of entertaining it is already something of an antithesis to the core element of Western philosophy of consciousness where conscious existential movement must be bounded by the domain of tangible, material causation.

The Accordance Principle “wears the mask almost of a contradiction” to materialist paradigms because it ascribes an intangible coupling between pre-existing counterfactual occurrence and the metaphysical fingerprint of a conscious observer. If a human is merely a materialist bag of water moving over the Earth, no such identifying fingerprint exists; but once the Accordance Principle is accepted, one is forced to reconcile which substrate mediates the reception of that fingerprint. The ad hoc dualist resolution would be to posit that a human has a soul directly and uses it as an antenna for communication with the Accordance Principle. Ajax’s preferred hypotheses non fingo resolution is to imagine starting a human’s consciousness at a “high potential clean slate” at the Null Aethus, with the Accordance Principle’s Aethic attributes having played out during a “genuine period of radically-widespread Aethic superposition content in one’s forgotten past” during early infancy. This paints the beginnings of a picture for what the Null Aethus really is.

404

Null Aethus as Pre-Infancy Superposition Unfolding

AethicOntologicalPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.1 footnote 178

Ajax’s specific proposal for what the Null Aethus actually represents in human developmental terms—a proposal that emerges from the Accordance Principle footnote rather than from the Null Aethus entry proper. The claim: the Accordance Principle does not operate retrospectively at the moment; instead, there is “a genuine period of radically-widespread Aethic superposition content in one’s forgotten past where the outcomes of the Accordance Principle concerning which natural historically Aethic attributes occurred relative to one’s Aethus actually unfolded dynamically and in real time.” This unfolding happens in early infancy, when fundamental features of known reality become Aethically accessible for the first time.

The proposal is significant for three reasons. First, it gives the Null Aethus a developmental location rather than leaving it as a purely mathematical starting point—the Null Aethus is the informational state a consciousness inhabits before the Accordance Principle’s selections have played out. Second, it dissolves the apparent nonlocality of the Accordance Principle by showing that the Aethic “decisions” happen in the observer’s own past Aethic time rather than retroactively; the selection is local once one has the right ontological framing. Third, it provides “the beginnings of a picture for what the Null Aethus really is” as a structural feature of framework development—the Null Aethus becomes “radically separate from any non-Aethic ontology” because it is characterized by a unique developmental dynamics rather than by mere emptiness. This is one of the rare places in the corpus where the biographical Null Aethus and the mathematical Null Aethus are brought into contact.

406

Accordance Principle as Earlier Pruning Agent

BothLogicalPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.12.1 footnote 130

The general methodological principle Ajax extracts from the grandfather paradox and quantum-suicide analyses: when a scenario requires a chain of improbabilities so extreme that the Third Postulate would have to intervene repeatedly, the Accordance Principle acts earlier and more efficiently by pruning the observer out of that scenario in the first place, before the improbability chain ever starts. The Accordance Principle is, in this sense, the “pre-filter” on experience—a logically-prior operation that keeps observers out of probabilistically-unmanageable Aethic trajectories rather than requiring per-step invalidation.

The conceptual relationship between Third Postulate and Accordance Principle matters because they are doing the same kind of work (pruning Aethae that would lead to contradiction or overwhelming improbability) at different altitudes of abstraction. The Third Postulate acts locally on Aethic descent from any given Aethus; the Accordance Principle acts globally on the anthropic selection of which Aethae an observer is likely to find themselves in. Ajax treats these as complementary rather than competing, and deploys the earlier-pruning-agent logic as a general diagnostic: whenever a paradox looks like it requires many Third-Postulate invalidations in sequence, ask whether the Accordance Principle dissolves the paradox earlier by making the starting scenario itself unreachable.

407

Death as Backwards Aethic Time Traversal

AethicPhilosophicalSpeculativeConcept
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 187

Ajax’s tentative, explicitly-flagged-as-speculative intuition about what death is in Aethic terms. The proposal: death is the mind’s way of moving backwards in Aethic time—at least in the sense of net displacement, “since god knows what happens in the middle.” He is careful not to establish the tie prematurely or literally (life and death are not simply “reflections in Aethic time”); rather, it would have to be something deeper and cleaner that “opens the door to backwards Aethic time traversal” or at least probabilistically enables it to a sense of non-negligence. The intuition follows from the observation that dying gives you added flexibility in some loose sense, with newfound access to the “lost multiverse” concept being a candidate manifestation.

The empirical source Ajax gathered the intuition from is near-death experiences themselves. Footnote 187 explicitly frames this: “Against my better judgment, maybe I will lay my current intuition on you of what death really is Aethically... at this point this is far closer to raw unfiltered intuitive speculation than anything formal or complete, so for goodness sake please take this with a grain of salt!” The self-restraint is as important as the content—Ajax is willing to gesture at the speculation but refuses to commit to it without the inductive bedrock his methodology requires elsewhere. It is one of the clearest places in the corpus where the author’s intellectual honesty about the boundary between speculation and derivation is explicitly marked.

408

Eccentric Unfolding (Extra-Centric Unfolding)

AethicPhilosophicalOntologicalConcept
Main paper §5 (centric unfolding section, footnote b)

The general category of any hypothetical alternative to standard centric unfolding. Centric unfolding is the default convention of Aethic reasoning—the assumption that an agent draws new attributes from their standing superposition at random and that every past Aethus serves as a parent Aethus to their current one. Ajax explicitly notes in the centric unfolding section that the convention is “only a convention” and that it “operates on substantially shakier ground than something like an Aethic postulate,” holding “a similar risk of invalidity to what the classical interpretation of the tricky principle held when we changed it to the second postulate of the Aethus.”

Anything departing from these two assumptions is what Ajax calls extra-centric unfolding. In a parenthetical footnote attached to the term, he observes that “perhaps fittingly, even if accidental on my part regarding the naming, these interpretations would indeed be quite eccentric in their furthered metaphysical peculiarity”—which is the source of the alternative name “eccentric unfolding.” The category exists in the framework as a placeholder for the methodological honesty that centric unfolding might one day be replaced, and it gives the framework a conceptual home for any specific variant proposed (relaxed monotonicity, alternative randomness selection, alternative parent-Aethus structure, or anything else that breaks the default axioms). Specific instances appear in the framework whenever the standard centric unfolding rules need to be loosened or modified to accommodate a particular phenomenon—most prominently the speculative non-monotonic variant proposed in the Maria–Clark NDE footnote, which loosens the monotonicity-of-Aethic-time-coordinate assumption to allow random attributes to be drawn from future child Aethae.

409

Simulation Hypothesis Dissolution

AethicPhilosophicalOntologicalResolution
Main paper §9.20

Ajax’s Aethic treatment of the simulation hypothesis (are we in a Matrix-style simulation, or perhaps dreaming?). In an objectivist or deterministic mindset, these problems hold a real status in the picture of reality and force a hard binary question about which “level” one occupies. In the Aethic mindset, the question dissolves rather than being answered. Given an iterative simulation stack with some “real world” on top and many nested simulations below, the philosopher’s instinctive question “which level am I on?” is itself an artifact of objectivism. By the Second Postulate, a person who is unaware of whether they are in a simulation must necessarily occupy all levels of the stack at once in Aethic superposition—perhaps even an agreeing superposition if an uncertainty-principle-like condition applies.

The existential follow-through: suppose someone found hard evidence they were in a simulation. In the objectivist mindset, this is existentially deflating because it demonstrates you are not living in the real world. In the Aethic mindset, the reaction is different: you are inherently just as real as the level above you, because you are real relative to your world and your Aethus, just as the other levels are real relative to themselves. “If we did indeed find ourselves in a simulation, we might simply readjust our perception and leave it at that. Or if anything we would not need to do any readjusting anyway, since by virtue of being computer characters, we would already be tailored to our environment no more or no less than we would have been before.” The First Postulate makes no Aethus superior to any other—the Aethic tree does not have a “top” in the existential sense. The framework is classified as a resolution (rather than merely a concept) because it explicitly dissolves a named philosophical dilemma rather than answering it within the dilemma’s original framing.

410

Flatworm Consciousness Thought Experiment

AethicThought ExperimentPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.21

Ajax’s Aethic approach to the hard problem of consciousness, introduced via a flatworm thought experiment. Consider an ancient ancestor similar to a flatworm with a rudimentary two or three thousand neurons that together enable calculated assessments of its oceanic environment—for example, gauging the basic salinity of the water and involuntarily swimming left or right accordingly. Mathematically, Ajax argues, just this simple mechanism is grounds for defining an Aethus: the ghost degrees of freedom of the salinity gauge can be treated as analogous, in their combination, to the Aethus corresponding to the system. The question of which specific attributes would depict this Aethus is daunting (especially in light of the Fundamental Theorem of Empirical Aethae), but the Aethus itself can be declared.

The move the thought experiment enables is this: by the Principle of Aethus-Reality Dualism, any Aethus automatically has a corresponding reality. So the flatworm’s neurological-salinity-gauge Aethus implies a reality for the flatworm relative to that Aethus. Biological life, by climbing the evolutionary ladder toward more sophisticated mechanisms of gathering information, automatically implies an Aethus rising in parallel with each advancement—which means the hard problem of consciousness becomes a question about which Aethae correspond to subjective experience rather than about how subjective experience arises from matter. The framing is significant because it converts the hard problem from an explanatory gap between physical and mental into an identification question within a framework that already treats both as Aethically mediated. Ghost Degrees of Freedom is the specific technical concept (#314) that makes the flatworm analogy precise rather than metaphorical.

411

Theory of Mind vs Aethic Intuition Tension

AethicMetaPhilosophicalEmpiricalConcept
Main paper §9.25 footnote 161

A specific empirical hypothesis Ajax raises in footnote 161, flagged with appropriate caution. Given that Aethic reasoning is deeply fundamental to reality, and given that autistic savants are both deep thinkers and comparatively immune to societal biases relative to an average allistic person, Ajax worries that “the parallel seems too apparent to miss.” The question he raises is whether any autistic savants have already solved the measurement problem in their own way but never conveyed the knowledge to neurotypicals—perhaps because a lack of standard theory of mind makes the conveyance structurally difficult. He notes it seems plausible “to imagine a scenario where a Level 3 autistic person naturally thinks in Aethic reasoning, and yet is merely diagnosed as lacking a theory of mind by a psychologist.”

The deeper hypothesis is that there may be a psychological phenomenon where a well-developed theory of mind and a generative intuition for Aethic reasoning actively conflict—that the cognitive machinery that makes a person good at modeling other minds is in tension with the cognitive machinery that makes a person good at letting go of the single-objective-reality assumption Aethic reasoning rejects. Ajax is explicit that given allistic humans have not yet traversed a measurement problem solution, “it is an intriguing if not ironic situation that those who have been diagnosed with a ‘disorder’ may have been more insightful on the topic than the psychologists diagnosing them.” The hypothesis is speculative and Ajax flags it as such, but it is one of the more consequential places where his framework raises testable cognitive-science predictions that are not merely restatements of the physics.

412

Four Control Groups for Aethic Innate Study

AethicMetaEmpiricalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §9.25

Ajax’s specific experimental proposal for testing whether humans are born with an innate tendency to favor the classical objectivist mindset or whether they are taught to do so by society. The proposal compares four control groups chosen to maximally differentiate the innate-vs-cultural question. (1) Allistic adults in a Western society—the default baseline. (2) Autistic adults in a Western society of various subcategories, treated as a potential control group that may be less influenced by standard societal bias. (3) Infant children younger than two years of age, as a direct probe of what humans think before socialization has had time to operate. (4) People of an indigenous or otherwise non-Western society, as a probe of whether the classical bias is specifically Western.

The logic behind the specific four-group comparison is that each pair between the four groups isolates a specific confound: autistic-vs-allistic isolates theory-of-mind development; infant-vs-adult isolates socialization-over-time; Western-vs-non-Western isolates the specific cultural package. By triangulating across the four, Ajax hopes to distinguish “the Aethic mindset of a sample” from “the innate Aethic mindset of a human”—two variables that are collapsed in any single-group study. The proposal is unusually concrete for a physics paper and indicates Ajax is willing to make his cognitive-science hypotheses falsifiable in a standard experimental-psychology format, even though the core framework lives in a different discipline.

413

Worldline Backtracking via Ladder Ontology

AethicMathematicalPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.12.1

The specific geometric picture that makes the Aethic Entropic Principle intuitive rather than paradoxical. Under classical objectivist assumptions, physically backtracking into your own past light cone and finding different circumstances than “what you left there last time” is either absurd or contradictory. The Aethic explanation: spacetime was never rigid in its contents to begin with. By the Union Principle’s ladder ontology, different Aethic block universes exist as arbitrarily-different perspectival viewings of the matter and energy within the same physical spacetime. The qualitative facet of the Extrusion Principle makes this concrete: space is “littered with” these different Aethic block universe informational layers inside the ladder ontology, so reversing one’s own worldline direction in time is simply one means of shifting between preexisting layers.

The picture also extends to closed timelike curves should they exist at all. The grandfather paradox resolution from the Entropic Principle is that the block universe at arrival must be different from the original block universe anyway, mediated by constant entropic increase relative to the observing agent’s Aethae. This is logically independent from the earlier Accordance-Principle resolution (which would prevent the trip in the first place)—the Entropic Principle handles the case where the trip does happen. The two mechanisms together form a layered defense: the Accordance Principle as early filter, the Entropic Principle as fallback if the early filter is bypassed, and the Third Postulate as the ultimate structural guarantee. Ajax does not insist on the order in which the layers fire; what matters is that paradoxes get handled somewhere in the stack rather than anywhere in particular.

415

“Sorting Into Pockets” of the Ladder Ontology

AethicPhilosophicalOntologicalConcept
Main paper §2 footnote 22

The specific metaphor Ajax uses in footnote 22 to describe what happens to an observer when they add information to their Aethus about a disagreeing superposition. You do not collapse the superposition in any causal sense—you “sort into the pocket” of the ladder ontology where one branch is permanently accessible to you and the other is permanently locked off. The ladder ontology metaphor matters because it distinguishes the Aethic process from three alternative pictures that all sound similar but are structurally different. It is not many-worlds branching (because the other branch still exists in the same physical spacetime, not in a parallel one). It is not wave function collapse (because nothing changes about the underlying physical reality, only your informational indexing). And it is not hidden-variables selection (because the other branch is not “the same reality with different hidden values,” it is a genuinely different Aethic block universe layered over the same physical substrate via the Extrusion Principle’s qualitative facet).

The metaphor’s usefulness is that it gives readers a single concrete image to anchor the otherwise abstract claim that physical “and” is not the counterpart of logical “and.” When you sort into a pocket, you are picking which logical relations you have access to while leaving the physical substrate invariant. Ajax flags this framing as especially useful for readers who want an alternative way into the Union Principle’s canonical table beyond the purely algebraic route.

416

Clark’s Verification as Maria’s Information Channel

AethicPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 187 (updated paragraph)

The striking structural claim that the updated end of footnote 187 makes about what information channel Maria’s veridical NDE actually runs through. Because there is no objective character to the shoe beyond the random set of versions coupled to Clark’s Aethae, and because Clark’s reduced form selects exactly the Maria-version whose testimony will not contradict her eventual verification, the very mechanism of Clark’s verification is what constrains which Maria-version appears. Ajax states it precisely: “strictly relative to Clark’s Aethus subsequently to hearing Maria’s attestation, at least for that Aethic multiverse-version of Maria it is none other than Clark’s future light cone-verification itself which serves as the channel of information through which Maria gained that information in the first place.”

The loop structure: Clark views the shoe in her future light cone → she confirms it with Maria in the present → this represents the original Aethic future point from which Maria (during her prior NDE) randomly grabbed the information → Maria then passes it back to her own Aethic-past self → she relays it to Clark. The counterfactual test Ajax offers is surgical: “Had Clark never checked for the shoe in the first place, then that informational channel back to Maria would have been severed, so she never would have known to bring it up in the first place, and instead perhaps would have selected something else.” The consequence is that the access point of the subject’s awareness during a veridical NDE is not outside the bounds of space and time; it is captured literally in the view of the verifying agent later on. Ajax’s compressed formulation: “It is as if her knowledge of the shoe came from her viewing it through Clark’s own eyes in her Aethic future, except in the sense of Aethic informational exchange rather than actual mental vision.” The caveat about informational-exchange rather than mental-vision is load-bearing because of the Aethic-truth-vs-mental-knowledge distinction the framework relies on.

417

Double-Slit / NDE Conspiring Parallel

AethicPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 187

Ajax’s explicit identification of a striking structural parallel between the double-slit experiment and the Maria–Clark NDE case—they both exhibit the same “apparent conspiring” phenomenon where the universe seems to resist letting experimenters separate the target system from the verification step. In the double-slit case, introducing a detector changes the back-screen pattern, so you cannot run the control case of “with verification vs without verification” while keeping everything else equal. In the Maria case, removing Clark’s verification would sever the channel that enabled Maria’s accurate description, so you cannot run the control case of “with verification vs without verification” there either.

The common Aethic root is the same reduced-form coupling mechanism operating in both systems: verification is not a passive read-off of pre-existing facts but an active participant in determining which Aethic branch the system lives in. The double-slit experiment is the quantum-scale instance; the Maria case is the biological-scale instance; but the structural logic that makes both experimental nightmares is identical. Ajax’s line: “The key commonality behind both of these cases is that they arise from the same Aethic effect. Or, that is the claim, anyway.” The parallel is significant because it lets Ajax treat the NDE interpretation not as a special supernatural-adjacent hypothesis but as a direct consequence of the same machinery already in place for the measurement problem—both cases fall under one unified treatment rather than needing separate theoretical apparatus.

418

Maria Falsifiability Criterion and Experimental Collapse

AethicMetaMethodologicalEmpiricalCriterion
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 187

Ajax’s explicit falsifiability criterion for his Aethic NDE mechanism, and the sharp observation that the criterion happens to be structurally unreachable by the same logic that makes it a criterion in the first place. He states it crisply: “The clearest way to falsify my idea on this topic is finding a sure case where accurate information is reported without being verified, because that contradicts that an information channel must have pointed backwards from verification so as to enable the accuracy in the first place.” If there exists even one veridical NDE case where the reported information is known to be accurate but no verification ever happened, the Aethic mechanism is false.

But the mechanism’s own logic immediately makes the criterion experimentally paradoxical: “By even knowing the information was accurate we must have proven so by verifying it, so the very substance of the experimental method collapses.” Any verification counts as the channel, so any case where we know the information is accurate is by definition a case where the channel was established. The criterion is well-defined but empirically self-undermining—which, notably, is exactly the same structural obstacle the double-slit experiment presents. Ajax explicitly points to this parallel: “Notice how this is precisely what makes the double-slit experiment such an experimental nightmare, and yet by inventing quantum mechanics we’ve had to accept it anyway, for the simple reason that it is the only conclusion which is mechanically possible in retrospect.” The falsifiability criterion is therefore methodologically serious—Ajax is honest about what would break his theory and why the break is not practically testable—and the honesty itself is a methodological signal about how the framework treats unfalsifiable claims.

419

Circular Pruning Rebuttal via Extrusion Principle

AethicLogicalMathematicalResolution
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 187

Ajax’s explicit response to a natural objection against the Maria–Clark mechanism: “Is Clark’s reduced form pruning of possible future Aethae not circular if the Aethus in the moment passes that pruning information to the future Aethae and is dependent on the future Aethae for what was pruned?” The worry is that the reduced-form coupling looks like it requires information to travel backwards in time, which would be either circular or an unacknowledged retrocausality.

The Aethic resolution runs through the Extrusion Principle. Clark’s verification event sits in the proper-time future relative to her meeting with Maria—but the meeting and the verification are both events within Clark’s own current block universe, indexed to her Aethus at the time of the meeting. The disagreeing superposition of possible events in the proper-time future is selectively pruned by the reduced form of the prior Aethus, not by the future Aethus acting back on it. “Already in the proper time future of Clark’s block universe the pruning has taken place in the Aethic sense, such that the disagreeing superposition in Clark’s own block universe of different things Maria relays and Clark sees in the proper time future has been pruned precisely sufficiently enough such that they must agree, as any event in the disagreeing superposition which fails to do so itself becomes checkmated under the third postulate.” Clark then centric-unfolds into one of the pre-pruned surviving states when she either hears Maria’s attestation or sees the shoe directly.

The logic is structurally identical to the observer consistency derivation from the Third Postulate: by the Indiscriminate Time Principle, it does not matter whether we examine Alice’s Aethus before, during, or after her measurement and comparison with Bob; the Aethic pruning operates over disagreeing superpositions in any proper-time past, present, or future of the block universe. The apparent circularity dissolves because the paradox assumed the future is monolithic, whereas the Extrusion Principle never required that assumption in the first place. The rebuttal matters because it shows that the veridical-NDE interpretation does not need retrocausality as an additional postulate—everything is handled by infrastructure already in place.

420

Newton-Einstein Biographical Inspirations

AethicMetaPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 189

Ajax’s explicit primary-source statement of the two historical figures who most shaped his methodology, given as a compact passage in footnote 189 of §10. Worth preserving as one of the cleanest windows into how the framework’s intellectual posture was formed: the footnote is short enough to reproduce in full, and it cleanly pairs the two influences with what specifically each one taught him.

Growing up, my two biggest inspirations were probably Newton and Einstein. Newton, because having learned the nature of his work at a young age, I realized that inductive reasoning is the closest thing we have to magic: in a matter of seconds it can turn the single most antirealist sentiment on the planet into boring clarity. Einstein, because he taught me that everything in the universe is fundamentally organized in relativism, and that it is not to be avoided but to be expected that a deeper physical paradigm will dismiss at least one key common sensical idea about the universe.

(And note, to be clear, the magic of inductive reasoning I mean is less the discovery process itself and more the sheer capacity to explanatorily build a worldview from nothing in the blink of an eye. Newton’s work embodies this with the cannonball thought experiment itself, which has an ability to reveal profound, intuitive truth in seconds. To this day it gives me chills, because it is something like the greatest single masterpiece in the history of thought experiments—the kind of thing that can serve as the very model of what conceptual physical science is aiming for when done right).

Four points worth drawing out of this passage. First, the two lessons are structurally distinct: Newton taught him about a methodology (inductive reasoning as near-magical worldview-building), while Einstein taught him about a posture toward ontology (relativism as the expected shape of any deeper paradigm, common-sense dismissal as a feature rather than a bug). This division shows up directly in the framework—the inductive methodology is how Aethic reasoning was derived, and the relativist posture is what the First Postulate formally commits it to. Second, the “not to be avoided but to be expected” framing for common-sense dismissal is the exact permission structure that lets Ajax propose something as radical as the Extrusion Principle without feeling he is being unreasonable—Einstein established the precedent, and Ajax is drawing on it. Third, the parenthetical clarification about inductive reasoning is significant: Ajax distinguishes between the discovery process itself (the moment of insight) and the downstream explanatory capacity (building a worldview in seconds), and it is specifically the latter he is pointing at. This is consistent with his repeated claim that he does not think in named principles but in “fluid conceptual interdependencies”—the inductive magic is in the framework’s ability to explain once it exists, not in the moment of having had it. Fourth, the cannonball-thought-experiment reverence is diagnostic of Ajax’s own aesthetic: he writes his own thought experiments (the Oliver soccer field, Emily and her father, the Maria–Clark NDE) in the Newtonian mode of trying to reveal profound truth in seconds via simple, personally-legible setups.

421

Newton’s Cannonball as Greatest Thought-Experiment Masterpiece

AethicMetaMethodologicalPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.28.3 footnote 189

A specific primary-source assessment Ajax offers in footnote 189 as a clarification of what he means when he says inductive reasoning is “the closest thing we have to magic.” He is not primarily referring to the discovery process but to “the sheer capacity to explanatorily build a worldview from nothing in the blink of an eye.” His exemplar: “Newton’s work embodies this with the cannonball thought experiment itself, which has an ability to reveal profound, intuitive truth in seconds. To this day it gives me chills, because it is something like the greatest single masterpiece in the history of thought experiments—the kind of thing that can serve as the very model of what conceptual physical science is aiming for when done right.”

The specific praise—“the greatest single masterpiece in the history of thought experiments”—matters because it identifies Newton’s cannonball as Ajax’s explicit standard for what thought experiments should aim for. The cannonball experiment takes a classical reader who has no prior reason to think orbital motion and terrestrial projectile motion are the same phenomenon and converts that reader, in a few seconds of imagination, into someone for whom the equivalence is obvious. That is the template Ajax is trying to match with Oliver’s soccer field, the sealed phone booth, the flatworm thought experiment, and the other Aethic thought experiments. Whether he succeeds is a matter for readers to judge; what matters for the historical record is that Ajax has named the gold standard he is aiming at, and it is a standard in which the thought experiment does the explanatory work rather than just illustrating a result reached elsewhere.

422

Leibniz Equality Pre vs Post Reversal Principle

AethicPhilosophicalLogicalConcept
Main paper §9.29 footnote biographical section

A particularly sharp statement of what the Reversal Principle does at the informational level, which Ajax articulates while recounting his own confusion in re-reading his original Accordance Principle Google Doc from 2020. The pre-reversal-principle alternative, he explains, would be to suppose that humans as a class require a perfectly matching set of all surrounding properties in order to remain at the same class. Under that mindset, so much as tweaking which species did or did not exist in history would cause us to lack Leibniz equality with ourselves—a butterfly-effect-style fragility in which no observer survives any counterfactual change to their environment.

The Reversal Principle’s move is to reject this all-information-needed ontology and replace it with a subset-of-information ontology. Ajax’s compressed framing: “The distinction between the pre and post reversal principle mindset comes down merely to ontologically suggesting that all information versus a subset of information is required to properly define an ontology.” This is what lets the Accordance Principle work at all—the Accordance Principle concludes that a human can still be a human at all if dinosaurs had not existed, and that conclusion is only available once one has rejected the all-information requirement. The characterization matters because it reduces the Reversal Principle to a single precise informational claim (partial specification suffices for ontological identity), which is far cleaner than the more common framings that rely on phenomenological or epistemic vocabulary.

423

Subconscious Reversion to Pre-Reversal Mindset

AethicMetaEmpiricalPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §9.29

A primary-source biographical episode Ajax records about the Reversal Principle’s fragility as a cognitive frame. After writing his original Accordance Principle documentation on June 9, 2020, he later re-read the Google Doc and experienced “an inexplicable confusion” about what the pitch was even saying. The explanation he gives in retrospect: in that moment he had subconsciously reverted back to the pre-reversal-principle mindset, even though he had clearly been at a reversal-principle-oriented mindset while writing the document in the first place. Under the reverted mindset, the Accordance Principle literally did not make sense—because the all-information-needed ontology forecloses the counterfactual comparisons the Accordance Principle requires.

A few months later, Ajax read the document yet again, by then having subconsciously reverted back to the reversal-principle mindset (and never reverted again after that). This was sufficient both to identify the correct interpretation and to understand where he had gone wrong in his prior reading. The added benefit, he notes, was that he then had a conscious understanding of the Reversal Principle for the first time—the accidental reversion-and-return gave him the triangulation point that a single steady mindset could not. Ajax explicitly flags the fragility: “Had this second reversion not occurred, it does not take much analysis to realize that my life would have likely played out very differently from there.” The episode matters because it documents that the Reversal Principle is not a stable default mental frame even for someone who has already articulated it formally—it requires deliberate maintenance or occasional accidental rediscovery. This is methodologically significant for anyone trying to assess why the framework took so long to articulate: the cognitive substrate the principle requires is unstable, so even people who reach it may lose it before they can write it down.

425

Aethic Base Principle of Superpositions (Principle 3)

AethicPhilosophicalLogicalPrinciple
Main paper §2 · Principle 3

The early-paper informal version of what will become the Second Postulate once the mathematical structure of the Aethus is in place. Stated formally: “an agent’s consciousness will always occupy whatever block universe in the Aethic Markov chain maximizes the surrounding superposition content of their universe without alerting them to a discernible difference between any two particular states in superposition.” Alternatively: where a block universe may be written as the superposition of some number of alternative block universes, an agent’s consciousness will occupy whichever block universe corresponds to the superposition of the set of all block universes whose variation in the moment the agent could not possibly be alerted to.

The principle matters structurally because it is the bridge between the Schrödinger’s cat analysis and the formal Second Postulate. Principle 3 is inductively motivated by the cat case—the experimenter at 2:00 PM can occupy the superposition of their own two subsequent-block-universe selves because there is no discernible difference in their environment to tip them off, but the cat itself cannot occupy that superposition because one component of itself is dead. When Ajax subsequently builds the Aethus structure, Principle 3 becomes the Second Postulate with a more mathematically objective grounding than a “supposition of consciousness.” Including this as its own entry matters for the historical record of how the Second Postulate was reached: it was reached via Schrödinger’s cat and a consciousness-placement induction, not via quantum formalism.

426

Parking Lot Disagreeing Superposition Example

AethicThought ExperimentConcept
Main paper §5.4.1

Ajax’s first “daily life” illustration of Aethic phenomena, used to introduce the disagreeing-superposition concept in a concrete and non-quantum context. The setup: consider a parking lot in which your friend has parked their car. If your back is turned to the parking lot, then relative to your Aethus, your friend’s car is in Aethic superposition over being parked in every single spot in the entire lot (because you are unaware which spot), plus in superposition with not having arrived at the lot at all (due to whatever delays may have held them up). Both superpositions are specifically disagreeing, as Ajax notes can be derived once the Third Postulate is in place.

The natural rebuttal: can’t you just turn around and see the parking lot, disproving the superposition claim? Ajax identifies this as a fallacy. The very act of turning and looking performs centric unfolding, transiting you to a new Aethus. Even if at that point you clearly see where your friend has parked, their car is still in superposition relative to your prior Aethus (before you looked), and relative to your new current Aethus, your friend “was always conveniently at the spot they are now parked in.” This dual statement—that both framings are simultaneously correct indexed to different Aethae—is Ajax’s most compact statement of how disagreeing superpositions work in practice. The parking lot example is significant because it demonstrates that Aethic phenomena are not limited to quantum scales: the same disagreeing-superposition structure operates over macroscopic classical situations whenever informational access is the relevant variable.

427

Imagination as Potentially Invalid Aethus

AethicPhilosophicalConcept
Main paper §5.4.1

A striking consequence Ajax draws from the parking lot analysis: the ordinary human act of imagining what is happening elsewhere in the world—behind closed doors, in other rooms, beyond your line of sight—is not actually part of your own reality because it contains extra hypothetical information your Aethus does not have access to. Relative to your actual Aethus, the behind-the-scenes location is in a “strange, more raw state of existence” that is a disagreeing superposition—and you can never see what such a thing looks like, because it does not have a look in the first place. Every time a person imagines what is happening elsewhere, they are not picturing their own reality but something that may in fact be an invalid Aethus due to lack of rigorous examination with the postulates.

Ajax flags this as “very well may be the single most jarring consequence of Aethic reasoning.” He is careful to clarify what the claim is and is not: this is not saying your friend’s reality is no longer real, but that “the particular attribution which has them at an objective location is not real.” There is an actual real situation at play—one in which your friend is in Aethic superposition relative to your Aethus at the moment. A related footnote Ajax offers as a hypothetical: since the human brain is incapable of picturing a logical contradiction in its ontological lens, a further question would be whether humans are capable of dreaming about invalid Aethae. An interesting hypothetical to ponder. The entry matters because it converts ordinary mental imagery from innocent mental simulation into a potential ontological error, which is one of the more striking cognitive-science implications of the framework.

428

Battle of Brémule as Historical Blank Attribute

AethicThought ExperimentConcept
Main paper §5.4.2

Ajax’s thought experiment demonstrating that historical events unknown to the observer occupy Aethic superposition relative to the observer’s current Aethus—even though the events happened hundreds of years ago in objective time. The specific case: the Battle of Brémule, fought in the high middle ages between the French and the English. Ajax notes upfront that most readers have not heard of it and do not know who won, and suggests that any reader who happens to know the answer should substitute some other obscure battle.

The argument: since the reader is unaware of the Battle of Brémule at the moment of reading, the Boolean attribute “who won” is registered as blank within their Aethus. The outcome is in Aethic superposition, with both sides winning at once. When Ajax tells the reader in the next paragraph that the English won, centric unfolding demands that the reader sees a random answer weighted by Bayesian priors over French-English medieval conflicts. The reader’s Aethus physically moves to a new reality where the outcome materializes to what they observe, and in that new Aethus “the English have always won the Battle of Brémule, and to this Aethus no superposition was ever present at all.” The classical hidden-variable interpretation (the English always won, the reader just found out) is rejected: the Aethic claim is that “only now have you made this information real relative to yourself.”

Why specifically Brémule? Ajax notes in footnote 77 that he deliberately chose an obscure battle rather than something foundational like the Battle of Gaugamela. The reason has to do with Nexic-attribute contamination: foundational historical events are very tied up with the Accordance Principle, so Aethically moving backwards to before learning them is not enough to remove them from your Aethus—you would have to remove all Nexic-dependent facts that deduce them as well. Obscure battles like Brémule appear to be “blunt skirmishes” without Nexic significance, so they are more cleanly extractable from the Aethus by backwards-Aethic-time movement and serve as a cleaner illustration of the Indiscriminate Time Principle. This methodological note about choice of example is itself significant as a statement of what the framework considers structurally clean versus entangled.

429

Zoe Password Time Travel

AethicThought ExperimentConcept
Main paper §5.4.3

Ajax’s narrative thought experiment for distinguishing classical from Aethic time travel ontology via a password-guessing scenario. The setup: protagonist Zoe has broken into the antagonist’s office, has narrowed the computer password down to four possible options, and has only one attempt before the alarm system goes off. But Zoe has a science-fiction ability: she can jump back in time to her own body by exactly one minute, altering the course of history by just that amount. The question: how many attempts will Zoe expect to need?

Classical answer: process of elimination. First attempt has a 1/4 chance of success; if wrong, rewind and try again with 1/3; then 1/2; then guaranteed on the fourth. Expected value: ~2.08 tries. Aethic answer: Ajax identifies a key fallacy in the classical reasoning. The classical argument assumes the password was “physically set in the past” and its information was therefore “added to the universe” at that time, whether accessible or not. In Aethic reasoning this is ontologically baseless because it is formulated independently from any particular Aethus. The correct Aethic picture: Zoe adds the instance of the correct password to her Aethus at the moment of checking, long after it was originally set. The spacetime location of the password-setting is fixed, but the Aethic time of inclusion is what matters for centric unfolding.

The consequence: when Zoe time-travels back to before her previous check, she reverts to an Aethus that does not contain the elimination information. If she types “ILuvDoomsday” again, the probability of it being correct is back to 1/4 rather than 0—the information of having eliminated it is immaterial because the Aethus does not possess it. Classically she can leverage all prior attempts; Aethically the information was “generated for her Aethus upon her learning about it,” so traveling back to before she gained the knowledge reinvigorates the full superposition. Even the actions of the past antagonist who set the password are in superposition until Zoe makes the discovery. Result: every rewind has a 1/4 chance of success, making the expected number of tries 4 (the expected value of a geometric distribution with p=1/4) rather than ~2.08. A clean numerical illustration of why Aethic and classical time-travel ontologies predict different observable outcomes—not just different interpretations of the same outcome.

430

Bank Robbery Time Travel

AethicThought ExperimentConcept
Main paper §5.4.6

Ajax’s more visceral illustration of Aethic-vs-classical time travel ontology. The setup (flagged with an apology for the violence): you walk into a bank and while waiting in line, a robbery occurs. The robbers bust in, call out for the bank manager (a middle-aged man), and shoot him when he doesn’t cooperate fast enough. You use your ability to rewind time by five minutes, to just before you walked into the bank, and re-enter.

Classical prediction: under complete-reality postulates, everyone in the bank is still the same person, and the robbers are still on their way—they were all born before your landing time in the past, and so here they will stay. Aethic prediction: you revert to your prior Aethus with no discrimination maintained between past and future attributes. The only thing you bring with you is your memories (otherwise it would not be a time travel story). When you re-enter the bank, every person in line is an entirely different person than before. The robbers never show up in the first place. The bank manager, now, is a thirty-year-old woman rather than the middle-aged man. It is just a calm day at the bank.

The Aethic explanation: the old manager was swapped out because you only met him within those five minutes, so his identity was among the attributes that were rerolled during centric unfolding. The bank robbery was rerolled too—it is quite uncommon, and you had no prior information about it in your five-minutes-ago Aethus, so the reroll produced a no-robbery outcome. Even the attribute “the robbers were coming a little ways behind you when you first walked into the bank” applies to a circumstance before the five-minute window but was gathered during the window, so it too was rerolled. The thirty-year-old female manager has a full life beforehand—you simply traveled to an alternate reality where the middle-aged male manager did not exist and she exists in his place. Footnote 84 is careful to clarify the anti-solipsism point: the original block universe does not cease to exist; you simply routed to a different subsequent block universe from the same prior one, exactly like choosing to visit Hoboken instead of New York City does not erase New York City.

431

Jenny’s Japanese and Aethic Butterfly Effect

AethicThought ExperimentConcept
Main paper §5.4.7

Ajax’s most existentially unsettling time-travel thought experiment, illustrating what the Aethic framework implies about personal identity under Aethic time travel. Setup: you have a childhood best friend, Jenny, whom you have known since you were both two years old. You “basically know everything there is to know about Jenny, and she you.” At Jenny’s 30th birthday party, she suddenly reveals during the candle-blowing moment that she is fluent in Japanese—breaking into conversation with your Japanese brother-in-law Tim. Afterwards she tells you the whole story: she secretly learned Japanese starting at age ten, moving from library books to YouTube to online classes.

The twist: two hours after the party, you accidentally activate the Aethic time-travel ability your colleague Zoe handed over to you after she quit spy work. It sends you back three hours, to ten minutes before Jenny blows out the candles but critically before you learn about her Japanese. When Tim says something in Japanese in this new pass, Jenny blinks. She doesn’t know Japanese. She never learned it. “A core feature of the Jenny you knew—that she knew Japanese from the age of 10—has now evaporated.”

The question the thought experiment raises: is this even the same Jenny as the one from the other block universe? And the progression Ajax draws from this is where the Aethic butterfly effect lives. Because even a three-hour rewind can alter events in Jenny’s life from twenty years ago (she “never learned” something she apparently had been learning since age ten), progressively rewinding further and further back would accumulate Aethic divergences more rapidly. By the Indiscriminate Time Principle, the rewind can reach any moment which you predate having learned about, no matter how scattered those moments are in actual chronological ordering. At each such branch point, forward centric-unfolding again could alter further elements of Jenny’s personality. “By simply choosing how far back in time you go, you can systematically extract and reroll any particular property of Jenny’s personality.” The Aethic butterfly effect differs from the classical one in that it is smooth rather than dichotomous—not “Jenny is or is not born” but rather “Jenny has a subtly different past or personality that diverges directly after the Aethus of information you had about her at the point of the rewind.”

432

Colorblindness Deuteranomaly Aethic Treatment

AethicThought ExperimentEmpiricalConcept
Main paper §5.4.5

Ajax’s autobiographical thought experiment applying Aethic treatment to his own deuteranomaly color-blindness (which he has had since birth—he cannot distinguish blue from purple, among other issues). The classical explanation is simple: he was in a universe where he had deuteranomaly from the moment he was born, regardless of awareness. His diagnosis at age 4 simply told him about a reality that was fixed all along. The Aethic alternative makes a more subtle argument using three temporal landmarks: age 3 (qualia of color vision present, but no comparison to other humans known), age 4 (diagnosis establishes the relation between him and fellow humans), age 10 (first learning about three cone receptors scientifically).

The Aethic argument: at age 3, before any external comparison, Ajax was in superposition across universes where humans typically have two, three, or four cones, and where he himself has two, three, or four cones. His qualia of color vision is fixed in the Aethus, but the cone configuration is blank and in disagreeing superposition because he has no information to distinguish the scenarios. Importantly, whatever cone count he actually has in any of these superposed universes, “that is what it feels like to have that many cones”—the qualia is stable across the cone-count superposition because the qualia is the observer-indexed fact and the cone count is the world-side fact.

At age 4, the diagnosis adds only relational information (he is colorblind relative to typical humans), not definitive cone-count information. Ajax formalizes this as the logical statement (X+B)(X+C)(Y+C), where X means “I have two cones,” Y means “I have three cones,” B means “typical humans have three cones,” and C means “typical humans have four cones”—each disjunction is a consistent way he could be “colorblind” relative to the typical population. This keeps multiple cone-count combinations in superposition even after the diagnosis. The thought experiment matters because it is Ajax’s cleanest non-quantum application of the Second Postulate to an ordinary epistemic situation with a time-ordered structure, and also because it uses his own life as empirical data—the biographical grounding is unusual for a physics paper but methodologically consistent with his stated preference for hypotheses non fingo reasoning from direct observation.

433

“Bustling Multiverse” of Overlapping Families

AethicPhilosophicalOntologicalConcept
Main paper §2

The specific ontological picture Ajax paints within the Emily–Sam thought experiment for what the house next door to young Emily actually contains, Aethically speaking. The picture is deliberately vivid: within the confines of that house is every possible family at once, “fading over one another without any knowledge whatsoever of the others.” For each possible family, there are pictures on the wall, laundry in the wash, and food in the kitchen. The point in spacetime that is the house’s living room is, from Emily’s Aethus, “essentially a bustling multiverse at which a vast density of timelines are bundled, all unbeknownst to one another.”

Ajax is precise about what this does and does not mean. It does not mean that billions of potential humans are crammed into the house spatially—that would be an absurdity and a misreading. It means that the house is an overlay of Aethic block universes each containing one family, with the different block universes sharing the same physical spacetime via the qualitative facet of the Extrusion Principle. Each family really is there relative to its respective Aethus. None of them is more real than any other until centric unfolding selects one. This framing is significant because it is Ajax’s most compressed explicit illustration of what “ladder ontology” means in everyday terms—not as an abstract algebraic claim but as something you could in principle walk into and either hear or not hear the laundry running. The “bustling multiverse” phrase has since become something of a trademark for the Aethic picture of disagreeing superpositions over classical scenarios.

434

Classical vs Aethic Time Travel Ontology Distinction

AethicPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §5.4

Ajax’s explicit generalization of what makes all the time-travel thought experiments (Zoe, bank robbery, Jenny’s Japanese) structurally different from their classical counterparts. The classical version of time travel requires an elaborate construct: one travels back to a past time but “meticulously crops out all attributes which occurred after the landing time” while “meticulously holding all attributes which occurred before the landing time in place.” Ajax compares this to the absolute value function—not analytic, not inherently fundamental, a piecewise construct rather than a natural operation.

The Aethic version is simpler and more unified: you revert to your prior Aethus with no discrimination between past and future attributes maintained. All attributes gathered during the rewound window are released back into their superpositions, regardless of whether they refer to events that chronologically predate or postdate the rewind point. This is why the Battle of Brémule can be “rerolled” even though it happened hundreds of years ago—the attribute was gathered by your Aethus recently, and the Aethic time of inclusion is what matters for centric unfolding, not the spacetime location of the underlying event. The generalization is significant because it exposes the classical time-travel ontology as an arbitrary piecewise construct where the Aethic treatment is a single uniform rule. Ajax flags the generalization as a “cautionary tale of sorts toward other areas in physics where the same phenomenon is still ongoing”—the suggestion being that other pieces of physics may harbor similarly arbitrary piecewise constructs that a more careful analysis would reveal as unnecessary.

436

§7 Slit Permutation Cases Table

AethicMathematicalLogicalResolution
Main paper §7 (Solution to the Double-Slit Paradox)

The explicit case-by-case Aethic resolution of the standard two-slit experiment, structured as a four-by-two validity table that drives the entire derivation of quantum interference and decoherence. Ajax decomposes the experimenter’s child Aethae into four exhaustive Slit Permutation Cases: Case 1 (neither slit is gone through), Case 2 (Slit A but not Slit B is gone through), Case 3 (Slit B but not Slit A is gone through), and Case 4 (both slits are gone through at once, in the Aethic sense of an agreeing superposition over slit-traversal). Each case is a child Aethus of the experimenter’s Aethus.

The validity output for the two detector configurations is precisely:

Detector running: Case 1 valid, Case 2 valid, Case 3 valid, Case 4 invalid. Detector absent: Case 1 valid, Case 2 invalid, Case 3 invalid, Case 4 valid.

This table is the main paper’s most compact statement of the Aethic resolution to the measurement problem. The detector-off result (Cases 2 and 3 invalid, Case 4 valid) explains why the interference pattern arises: the only Aethae compatible with Heisenberg’s uncertainty principle keeping the which-slit attribute blank are Case 1 and Case 4, and Case 4 is what produces interference. The detector-on result (Case 4 invalid, Cases 1, 2, 3 valid) explains why the clump pattern arises: with the detector available, Case 4’s agreeing superposition is checkmated by the Third Postulate because the which-slit attribute would eventually have to be determined, producing a contradiction with the “both at once” assertion. The same three Aethic postulates and the same Heisenberg input produce both empirical patterns from the same starting Aethus, with no quantum formalism beyond the uncertainty principle assumed.

Footnote 99 is structurally important: the four cases themselves are allowed to be in agreeing superposition within their total Aethus due to the Extrusion Principle. The reason is subtle—deciphering which of Case 2 or Case 3 happens with the detector running is found out only in a child Aethus to the Aethus in which it was blank, so the four cases can coexist in agreeing superposition without triggering contradiction in the parent Aethus. Footnote 100 clarifies that Case 4 does not trigger an Aethic contradiction in itself because it is the natural state of an Aethus where the which-slit attribute is simply blank and returns the characteristic agreeing superposition; Cases 2 and 3 are the “weird” ones because they hold the extra condition that one slit is definitively not gone through.

437

Heisenberg Uncertainty Principle as Aethic Input

AethicEmpiricalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §7.1 (Derivation for the Detector-Off Case)

The role Heisenberg’s uncertainty principle plays in the §7 derivation as an empirical input to the Aethic logic, not as something the Aethic framework derives or replaces. Specifically, Ajax characterizes HUP as providing a “lemma” that “quantum information, once not measured, may never again be attained.” This is the empirical fact that the Aethic postulates use as their seed condition; the postulates do not themselves entail it but use it to determine which of the four slit permutation cases survive validity pruning.

The methodological significance is structural: the Aethic derivation of the double-slit pattern is not from-scratch quantum mechanics but is rather a derivation of decoherence and interference given Heisenberg’s uncertainty principle as input. This positions the Aethic resolution as a logical-relational reformulation of how HUP cashes out empirically, rather than as a competing physical theory at the quantum-formal level. In the detector-off case, HUP being in effect means that any valid child Aethus of the experimenter must hold the which-slit attribute as conceptually blank, forcing it into superposition by the Second Postulate—and Cases 2 and 3 are then invalidated because they fail to maintain that superposition. In the detector-on case, HUP no longer protects the which-slit attribute from determination, so the Third Postulate becomes available to invalidate Case 4. The framework’s honest acknowledgment that it leans on HUP rather than deriving it is itself a methodological transparency move—the Aethic claim is about the logical structure that connects HUP to the empirical patterns, not about replacing HUP with something more fundamental.

438

Mozart Questionnaire and Double Descent Aethic Explanation

AethicPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §5.3 (Aethic Reasoning and the Philosophy of Machine Learning)

Ajax’s extended thought experiment using Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart as the test subject for an Aethic interpretation of double descent in machine learning. The setup: how does one, to philosophical accuracy, describe a person like Mozart? Ajax walks through the natural language attempt—“He is artistic. He is profound but also somewhat childlike. He embodies leadership while also being an outcast”—and notes that each word evokes a feeling that compresses an otherwise vast configuration space of possible characters. Then he asks the harder question: how would one pass such nuance to a computer in place of a human?

The argument starts at the bit level. Suppose we set up dichotomies: “artistic vs. calculating,” “profound vs. boring,” “leader vs. follower,” etc. Already trouble appears—by declaring “artistic vs. calculating” as a binary, we implicitly assume calculating is the antonym of artistic, which adds a stricter definition of artistic that was never intended. As we instate more dichotomies, more inaccuracies creep in, and eventually we are hardly describing Mozart at all. Adding finer resolution to each dichotomy (four bits per dichotomy giving sixteen levels) only slightly slows the loss of accuracy. From five to ten to twenty dichotomies, the correlation between the data set and physical Mozart drops.

Then the pattern reverses. At eight thousand dichotomies, a new phenomenon takes over: the probability of ever coincidentally re-entering the same parameters becomes almost surely zero. For two independently answered questionnaires about Mozart, the probability of their being equal across all responses is unlikely on the order of quantum fluctuations. But more importantly, for two questionnaires about different people, the probability of their vectors being equal is even lower—exponentially so. By Bayes’ theorem, this means that if you encounter two questionnaire vectors that match, the probability they describe different people is vanishingly small. The vector A_Q sufficiently “captures” the essence of A—in this case captures the essence of Mozart to any degree requested of it. The degrees of freedom needed to describe Mozart have been written, but not plainly—they are abstractly contained in the data.

The Aethic interpretation: this is double descent operating as a manifestation of the categorical abstraction. Below the threshold, the parameters are still trying to play a Cartesian role—each bit is supposed to compartmentalize a feature. Above the threshold, the parameters have crossed into an extra-Cartesian regime where they are no longer compartmentalizing but are instead acting as a Galois-group-like abstract whole that captures Mozart precisely because no individual parameter has to mean anything by itself. The Aethic framework predicts this transition because it is exactly the move from epistemic-Aethus reasoning (where stated-attributes are decomposable) to empirical-Aethus reasoning (where the categorical abstraction takes hold and indecomposable richness becomes the source of strength). It is one of the framework’s clearest predictive applications outside physics.

439

Late Wittgenstein Convergence on Aethic Retrievals

AethicPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §5.2.2

Ajax’s observation that empirical-Aethus retrievals over semantic constructs like “Dog” converge with late Wittgenstein’s philosophy of language. The setup: in a purely epistemic Aethus, asking “what kind of pet does Tim own?” returns “Dog” because the attribute is state-decomposable. But in the empirical case, “Dog” is not actually a well-defined Aethic state under the Fundamental Theorem of Empirical Aethae—it is a semantic construct that cannot be decomposed into axiomatic parts while maintaining its complex-emergent character. What gets retrieved instead is “an utterly complex multi-nested system of Aethic superpositions which topologically or otherwise structurally are consistent with what we semantically call Dog.”

The Wittgensteinian alignment: late Wittgenstein attributes semantic words to a vast graph-like structure of indecomposable relationships rather than to any naive semantic primitive, and he forces the first principles of language to only ever concern relativist familial resemblances between word-structures rather than any objective or intrinsic nature of individual words. Ajax notes the convergence is striking because the Aethic framework reaches the same conclusion through a completely different route—not from linguistic analysis but from the categorical-abstraction-derived structure of empirical Aethae. Footnote 75 makes a further methodological observation: a world where words must be individually objective has more constraints (in degrees-of-freedom terms) than the relativist view, because the relativist view discards the subset of constraints that fix objective word-meaning. This is loosely analogous to the throwing-out of the degree of freedom for an objective position within Galilean relativity. The convergence is a side benefit of the categorical-abstraction move—Aethic reasoning, by accepting the indecomposability of empirical Aethae as a strength, ends up validating one of late Wittgenstein’s central moves on its own grounds.

440

Mozartian Human Intuition as Galois Group of Individual Contribution

AethicMetaPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §2.2.9 footnote on individual scientific contribution

Ajax’s structural claim that the unique contribution of individuals to ontological breakthroughs in physics has the same shape as a Galois group acting on the roots of a quintic—an irreducible whole that cannot be compartmentalized or distributed across many people, but that nonetheless manifests as a definite mathematical object. The context is Ajax’s broader Kuhnian-adjacent argument that individuals are necessary for sparking paradigm shifts while the collective is necessary for normal science between them, with neither being more important in absolute terms but each playing structurally distinct roles.

The specific Mozartian framing: just as Mozart’s K. 361 III. Adagio (Ajax’s Mozart Indecomposability Hypothesis case) is structurally indecomposable in the sense that it cannot be assembled from prior compositional intuitions but had to be intuited as a complete Galois-group-equivalent whole by a single mind capable of holding its complexity in one thought, so too are ontological breakthroughs in physics. Ajax notes that “you have never exactly had an ontological breakthrough in physics which was done by the full collective.” This is not a moral claim about individual versus collective importance but a structural claim about what kind of cognitive object an ontological breakthrough is. The historical evidence Ajax points to is the contrast between the relativistic revolution and the quantum revolution: special and general relativity were ontological masterpieces produced by a single individual, while quantum mechanics is “anomalous in the history of physics for its sheer vacancy of ontological content” despite being mathematically mature and produced collectively. The framing matters because it gives Aethic reasoning’s biographical content (the journal entries, the soccer field, the sidewalk coin flip) a structural justification: if ontological work has Galois-group cognitive shape, then the autobiographical record of how a single mind reached the framework is itself part of the methodology, not a personal indulgence.

441

Einstein vs Dirac Ontological Asymmetry Diagnostic

AethicPhilosophicalMethodologicalConcept
Main paper §2.2.9 footnote

Ajax’s specific historical comparison used to diagnose what makes a physics breakthrough ontologically complete versus ontologically vacant. The Dirac equation, Ajax notes, is “easily as mathematically elegant as Einstein’s special relativity, and arguably more so due to its further level of fundamentality.” And yet Einstein fits the Newtonian characterization much better than Dirac despite the two being “on par” in raw brilliance. The difference is not mathematical elegance or fundamentality but explicit ontological content: Einstein’s special relativity is explicit in its ontological implications, while the Dirac equation, like the rest of quantum mechanics, leaves its ontological implications unsaid.

This is the diagnostic Ajax uses to argue that quantum mechanics is “anomalous in the history of physics for its sheer vacancy of ontological content”—not because it lacks mathematical depth but because it consistently ducks the ontological commitments other physics theories make. Ajax connects this to the inevitability thesis (whether someone else would have discovered relativity had Einstein not): “genuinely I think it would have been quite a long shot for either” special or general relativity. General relativity in particular “remains, a full century later, a work whose ontological depth has arguably not been matched by any subsequent advance in physics—and this stubborn singularity in the historical record is itself evidence that the capacity to produce such work is extraordinarily rare.” The diagnostic functions as Ajax’s own implicit benchmark for what Aethic reasoning is trying to be: ontologically explicit in the way relativity is, rather than ontologically vacant in the way standard quantum mechanics is.

D

Empirical Postulates, Lemmas & Narrow Derivations

Formally numbered postulates, lemmas, definitions, and specific empirical claims that do supporting work in particular derivations but do not generalize beyond their immediate context.
103

Postulate of Unlikely Dinosaurian Demise

NexicEmpiricalPostulate
Postulate 1 of Nexic paper (§6.3)

The dinosaurs were unlikely to go extinct in such a way that the mammals survived—approximately 13% per peer-reviewed empirical assessment, based on the geographic specificity required for the Chicxulub impact to produce sufficient devastation while leaving a viable mammalian niche. Grounds the first branch of the two-node dinosaurian circumstance that originally seeded Nexic reasoning in 2018—the second branch being the prerequisite that mammals (and eventually humans) arose at all. The 13% figure is what gives the dinosaurian extinction its status as a Miracle in Ajax’s technical sense: it satisfies the cosmic-rarity criterion (the K–Pg impact configuration is genuinely improbable) and the anthropic-prerequisite criterion (without it, no humans).

This entry is the structural hub of the entire dinosaur-Optimation sub-tree of Nexic reasoning. After the second-generation un-flattening pass it absorbed all the major dinosaur lemmas as direct children: the Principle of Dinosaurian Dominance, the Postulate of Absolute Dinosaurian Extinction, the Lemma of Dinosaurian Extinction Independence, the Lemma of Dinosaurian Existential Independence, the Lemma of Dinosaurian Late-Occurrence Probability, the Fundamental Probabilistic Context of Dinosaurs, the Significance of the Mass of Chicxulub, Dinosaurian Prosperity Before the K–Pg, the Threatening vs Avian Dinosaur distinction, the Mesozoic Niche Explosion Post K–Pg, the Theropod Anatomical Optimization Claim, and Dinosaurian Clade Reintroduction. Each of these operates inside the analytic frame this Postulate sets up—all of them are in some sense different ways of formalizing or quantifying what “unlikely demise of dinosaurs while preserving the mammals” actually means. Despite its D-tier status as a narrow empirical postulate (rather than a foundational principle), the entry plays the structural role of a hub because the dinosaur sub-tree is the canonical paleontological worked example for the entire Nexic apparatus.

104

Principle of Dinosaurian Dominance

NexicEmpiricalPostulate
Postulate 2 of Nexic paper (§6.3)

If dinosaurian dominance had remained, the probability of mammals beyond the size of a dog (or with highly variant niches) would be very low, making humanity next to nothing. Grounds the claim that the extinction of the dinosaurs is an anthropic parameter. Strong empirical support from the Mesozoic fossil record and the post-extinction mammalian niche explosion.

105

Postulate of Absolute Dinosaurian Extinction

NexicEmpiricalPostulate
Postulate 6 of Nexic paper (§8.4)

The direct effects of the K-Pg extinction event—the several minutes of broiling temperature and whatever else occurred—were entirely sufficient to wipe out the non-avian dinosaurs with a very high degree of likelihood. Used in the Dinosaurian Prosperity Before K-Pg analysis.

106

Lemma of Dinosaurian Extinction Independence

NexicMathematicalLemma
